\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F=\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}F),

where
\int
is defined in
1.86
. A morphism
f:d\to d^{\prime}
is taken to the functor

\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}F)\to\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}F)

which takes an object
(c\in\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{C}},x\in F(c)[d^{\prime}])
in
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\int_{\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}F)
to
(c\in\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{C}},F(c)[f](x)\in F(c)[d])
and is defined analogously on morphisms.

Notice the construction of
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F
gives us a functor

\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to\mathrm{op}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{roth}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}.

Indeed, by
3.5
, we have an isomorphism
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}:\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C},\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})))
and so it suffices to construct a functor
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C},\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})))\to(\mathrm{op}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{roth}_{\mathcal{D}})_{/\mathscr{C}}
. We have already defined it for objects (
3.8
). Now for a morphism
\alpha:F\to G
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C},\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}(\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})))
, we define
\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha:\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F\to\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G
level-wise for an object
d
in
\mathcal{D}
as
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\alpha)}

Here we note that
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\alpha:\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}F\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}G
is a natural transformation and so
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\alpha)
is the natural transformation defined as the composition
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\alpha}

.

Let us compute some important examples.

Example 3.9
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category and
c
an object in
\mathscr{C}
. Then we can define the corepresentable functor
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-):\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
, which takes an object
c^{\prime}
to the enriched hom object
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,c^{\prime})
. Using
1.87
we can now compute
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-)\to\mathscr{C}
as follows. For a given object
d
in
\mathcal{D}
we have

\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,-)=\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,-))=\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}(c,-)=(\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C})_{c/}.

Example 3.10
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category and
G
an object in
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
. Then the constant functor
\{G\}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
is an object in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
and we have
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\{G\}=\mathscr{C}\times\{G\}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathscr{C}
.

We can use this last example to get an enrichment.

Lemma 3.11
.

The functor
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}
is a
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor.
Here
\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
has the
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enrichment given in
1.23
and
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}
the
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enrichment given in
3.2
.

Proof.

As explained in
1.23
and
3.2
both enrichments are given via tensor, which is the level-wise product, which, as shown in
3.10
, commutes with the functor
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
. Hence, the results follows from
1.24
.
∎

Before we generalize our construction to spaces, we also need a right adjoint. We can construct one using the previous example, which motivates the functorial over-category construction.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. Define the
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{/-}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}
as the composition of the enriched Yoneda embedding (
1.28
)
\mathscr{C}\to\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C}^{op},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
with
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}^{op},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}
. We similarly define
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{-/}:\mathscr{C}^{op}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}
.

We can use this functor to finally define the right adjoint.

Definition 3.13
.

Define the
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
as the adjoint (using
1.32
) to the following composition map
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}\times\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}})^{op}\times\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{-/})^{op}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{/\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}}(-,-)}

,

where the first map is the opposite of
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{-/}
(
3.12
) and the second is the enriched mapping object (
1.29
).
In other words
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p)(c)=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{/\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{C}_{c/},\mathscr{D})
.

Notice the similarity of
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
with
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{C}}
(
1.89
), which justifies the notation we have chosen. We now want to prove that
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\vdash\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
gives us an adjunction, similar to
\int_{\mathscr{C}}
and
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{C}}
.

Theorem 3.14
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. Then we have a
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}

where the left adjoint
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
preserves (set-enriched) limits and is fully faithful with essential image
\mathrm{op}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{roth}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
.

Proof.

First, observe that
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
evidently preserves (set-enriched) limits and colimits in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{C}}
, as they are evaluated point-wise, where
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
simply coincides with the Grothendieck construction for categories, which we already know preserves limits and colimits (
1.89
). Moreover, by
3.11
,
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched and in particular preserves tensor and so, by
1.30
, preserves
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched colimits. Knowing that
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
preserves colimits means that in order to get an unenriched adjunction it suffices to prove that for every object
c
and object
p:\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at})_{/\mathscr{C}}
there is a natural isomorphism

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-),\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p))\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{/\mathscr{C}}(\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-),p)=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{/\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{C}_{c/},p)=\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p)(c),

which follows from the Yoneda lemma (
1.27
). Notice, by
1.26
and the fact the left adjoint commutes with the tensor (
3.10
), the adjunction is in fact a
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched adjunction. Finally as the essential image of
\int_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}
are precisely the Grothendieck opfibrations (
1.92
), the essential image of
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
are precisely the
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck opfibrations (
3.6
).
∎

Remark 3.15
.

Notice, we could have deduced the existence of an un-enriched right adjoint of
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
from the fact that both categories are locally presentable, however, the benefit of this approach is an explicit description of the right adjoint.

We will end this subsection with an analysis of the
universal
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck opfibration
, generalizing
1.96
. First of all, following
1.93
, the universal
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck opfibration is given via the projection map
\pi_{*}:\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{D[0]/}\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
and we again have the following result.

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. The functor

\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}(-)^{*}\pi_{*}:\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to\mathrm{op}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{roth}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}

given by pulling back
\pi_{*}
is precisely the functor
\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
and so gives us an equivalence between
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functors and
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck opfibrations over
\mathscr{C}
.

Notice, again
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck fibrations are stable under pullback (
2.10
) and applying
1.94
level-wise we obtain the pseudo-functor

\mathrm{op}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{roth}_{\mathcal{D}/-}:\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\widehat{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}}),

and again applying
[
Joh02a
, Theorem B1.3.6]
leads to the following definition analogous to
1.95
.

Definition 3.17
.

Let
\mathrm{op}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{roth}_{\mathcal{D}}
be the category with objects
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck opfibrations and morphisms pullback squares and notice it comes with a target projection functor to
\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
.

We now have the following result generalizing
1.96

Proposition 3.18
.

The pullback functor

\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}(-)^{*}\pi_{*}:(\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})})_{/\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}\to\mathrm{op}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{roth}_{\mathcal{D}}

is an equivalence of
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor over
\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
.

Proof.

First of all for a
\mathcal{D}
-set
X
and
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck opfibration
\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
, the composition
X\times\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
is again a
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck opfibration. Hence the tensor on
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}))
(as described in
3.2
) restricts to a tensor on
\mathrm{op}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{roth}_{\mathcal{D}}
, making it into a
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. Moreover, pulling back along
\pi_{*}
respects products with
\mathcal{D}
-sets and so the functor
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}(-)^{*}\pi_{*}
is
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched.

Using the same argument as in
1.96
the equivalence follows from
3.14
and
3.16
.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

3.18
implies that for
\mathcal{D}
-Grothendieck opfibration
\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}
, there is a functor of
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories
F:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
and a pullback square of
\mathcal{D}
-categories of the form
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{D[0]/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}F}

unique up to equivalence.

3.2.
Grothendieck Construction for
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-Enriched Categories

We are now in a position to generalize the construction from
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories to
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories, which is the goal of this subsection. Notice, there is an isomorphism of categories
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})\cong\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
and so we could simply use that to generalize all our previous constructions from
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories to
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories. However, the additional simplicial direction should help us obtain homotopical properties and in particular construct model structures and Quillen equivalences, which requires us to adjust certain constructions.

Following
3.3
define the functor

\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}:\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}).

For the next definition we use the fact that we have an (unenriched) isomorphism of categories
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})\cong\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
.

Definition 3.20
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category.
Define
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
as the composition of
\int_{\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}/\mathscr{C}}
and the nerve functor.

Remark 3.21
.

By direct computation and
3.8
, the
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G
is level-wise equal to

(s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G)[d,k]_{l}=\coprod_{c_{0}\to\cdots\to c_{k}\in\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d,l}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d,l}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],G(c_{0}))

with the map
(s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G)[d,k]_{l}\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}[d,k]_{l}
being the evident projection to
c_{0}\to...\to c_{k}
.

Alternatively, we can only fix
k
and observe that we have an equality of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces

(s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G)[-,k]=\coprod_{c_{0},...,c_{k}\in\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{C}}}G(c_{0})\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c_{0},c_{1})\times...\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c_{k-1},c_{k})

The computation has the following valuable implication regarding
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
.

Lemma 3.22
.

The map
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
is a
\mathcal{D}
-left morphism (
2.2
) and so in particular the injective fibrant replacement is a
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration (
2.3
).

Let us do a sample computation similar to
3.9

Example 3.23
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category and
c
an object in
\mathscr{C}
. Then we can define the corepresentable functor
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,-):\mathscr{C}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
, which takes an object
c^{\prime}
to the enriched hom object
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,c^{\prime})
. Using an argument similar to
3.9
we have

s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,-)=N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{c/}.

Example 3.24
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category and
G
an object in
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
. Similar to
3.10
we have
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\{G\}=N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}\times\{G\}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathscr{C}
.

We also have the following useful observation.

Lemma 3.25
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category.
The functor
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
is
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched and commutes with
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-colimits and set-enriched limits.

Proof.

By
1.24
, to observe that
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched it suffices to observe that for every
F
in
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
we have
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\{F\}\times G=F\times s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G
, which simply follows from
3.24
. As we have shown preservation of tensors, by
1.30
, in order to show
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-colimit preserving it suffices to prove that the underlying functor preserves colimits.

Now, recall that (co)limits in
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
are evaluated level-wise. Fix
(d,k,l)
in
\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}
. Then the functor

s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(-)[d,k]_{l}:\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}

takes a functor
G
to the set
\coprod_{c_{0}\to\cdots\to c_{k}\in\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d,l}\mathscr{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d,l}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(D[0],G(c_{0}))
(
3.21
), which is a coproduct of a composition of an evaluation functor (at
c_{0}
) and a corepresentable functor, all of which are colimit preserving.
∎

We want to show that
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is a left Quillen functor, which requires a right adjoint.

Notation 3.26
.

In
3.12
we introduced the
\mathcal{D}
-category
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{/-}
and similarly
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{-/}
for a given
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category
\mathscr{C}
. Using the analogous construction for
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category
\mathscr{C}
and applying the nerve gives us
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces
N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{-/}
(
N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{/-}
). Recall, in
1.33
we defined the
\mathcal{D}
-nerve
N_{\mathcal{D}}:\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
, which in particular satisfies
N_{\mathcal{D}}=N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}
(similar to what we observed in
3.3
). Hence, we will simply denote the
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space
N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{/-}
by
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/-}
and
N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{-/}
by
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{-/}
.

Remark 3.27
.

Notice, similar to
3.12
, we get
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functors
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{-/}:\mathscr{C}^{op}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
and
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/-}:\mathscr{C}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}

We can now define
s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
analogous to
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
(
3.13
).

Definition 3.28
.

Define the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor
s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
as the adjoint (using
1.32
) to the functor
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}\times s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}})^{op}\times s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{-/})^{op}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}}(-,-)}

,

where the first map is the opposite of
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}_{-/}
(
3.27
) and the second is the enriched mapping object (
1.29
).
Concretely, for a given map
Y\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
we have

(3.29)

s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(Y\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})(c)=\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{c/},Y).

We are finally in a position to prove that the functors
(s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}},s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}})
give us a
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched adjunction between
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
and
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
.

Lemma 3.30
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. There is a
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

Proof.

Notice we proved in
3.25
that
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched colimit preserving functor. Hence, the result follows from the fact that
\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
is the free
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched cocompletion (
1.31
), meaning
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is the left Kan extension along the enriched Yoneda embedding and the right adjoint must take a map
Y\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
to a functor with value
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{c/},Y)
, which by
3.29
is precisely
s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
.
∎

We now move on to the next step: Constructing a second adjunction between
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
and
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
, where the left (and right) adjoint goes the other way.

Definition 3.31
.

Define the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
as the adjoint (using
1.32
) to the functor

s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\times\mathscr{C}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/-}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\times s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\xrightarrow{-\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}-}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})\xrightarrow{\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}).

Here the first functor is
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched by
3.27
, the second by
1.25
and the last because
\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}
preserves tensors and
1.24
. In other words
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p:Y\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})(c)=\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(Y\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/-})
.

Remark 3.32
.

The definition of
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
should be seen as the correct homotopical generalization of
\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{C}}
(
1.89
), as the direct generalization of
\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{C}}
to
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories would not have the desired homotopical properties.

The description suggests an alternative characterization. Notice, for a given
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category we have the following commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\times s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\times s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}\times\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}}

,

which gives us the following alternative characterization of
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
.

Lemma 3.33
.

The functor
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
defined as the adjoint

s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\times\mathscr{C}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/-}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\times s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\xrightarrow{\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}\times\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}}

s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\times s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\xrightarrow{-\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}-}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}).

is equal to
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
and so in particular we have
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p:Y\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})(c)=\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(Y)\times_{\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})}\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/-})
.

Notice
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
behaves as expected.

Example 3.34
.

Let
A
be a
\mathcal{D}
-space and
\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A)\to D[0]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
a map of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces. Then
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A))=\{A\}\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c,-)
, where
c
is the image of the point in
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
. Indeed, for a given object
d
we have

s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A)\to D[0]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})(d)=\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A))\times_{\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})}\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/d})=

A\times(D[0]\times_{\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})}\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/d}))=A\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c,d)

Let us look at another example.

Example 3.35
.

Fix a map
p:F[d,n]\times\Delta[l]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
and notice this map corresponds to a chain of
n+1
objects
c_{0}\to c_{1}\to...\to c_{n}
in the category
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}_{l}
. We want to understand the functor
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(F[d,n]\times\Delta[l]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}):\mathscr{C}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
. Fix an object
d^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{D}
and notice we have the equality of simplicial spaces
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}F[d,n]=\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}F[n]
and so in particular the map
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}F[d,n]\times\Delta[l]\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
corresponds to a map of simplicial spaces
\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}F[n]\times\Delta[l]\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
. We can now compute

\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p)=\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}((F[d,n]\times\Delta[l])\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/-})=

\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}F[n]\times\Delta[l]\times_{N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}}N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}_{/-})=\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(F[n]\times\Delta[l]\times_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}_{/-})

Notice again the map
F[n]\times\Delta[l]\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
corresponds to a chain of morphisms
c_{0}\to...\to c_{n+1}
in the category
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}_{l}
. The map
<0>:F[0]\to F[n]\times\Delta[l]
is a covariant equivalence over
N\mathscr{C}
by definition and so, by
1.103
, for every object
c
the map of simplicial spaces
F[0]\times_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}_{/c}\to F[n]\times\Delta[l]\times_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}_{/c}
is a covariant equivalence and so, by
[
Ras17b
, Theorem 3.17]
, gives us the diagonal equivalence

\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-)\xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ }\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(F(n)\times\Delta[l]\times_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}_{/-}).

Using the same argument as in
1.107
, this equivalence has an inverse that takes chains of morphisms
c_{0}\to c_{1}\to...\to c
to the composition
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},c)
giving us a diagonal equivalence

\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(F(n)\times\Delta[l]\times_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}N\mathscr{C}_{/-})\overset{\simeq}{\twoheadrightarrow}\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-).

However, the space
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-)
inside the coproduct does not depend on the choice of morphism in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)
. Hence we can separate them and get

\coprod_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(d^{\prime},d)\times\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-)=F[d]\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-)[d],

which proves that we have a level-wise equivalences of
\mathcal{D}
-space valued functors

F[d]\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-)\xrightarrow{\simeq}s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p)\xrightarrow{\simeq}F[d]\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-)

composing to the identity.

The goal is to show that
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is part of an adjunction.

Lemma 3.36
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. We have a
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N\mathscr{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

Proof.

We first show it is an unenriched adjunction and then prove the adjunction is in fact enriched. The functor
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
is colimit preserving. Indeed, colimits in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
are evaluated level-wise and for a given object
c
the functor
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(-)(c):s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
is given via the composition of the pullback functor
-\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/c}
and
\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}
, both of which are colimit preserving.

This implies that
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is given as the left Kan extension of the functor
(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N\mathscr{C}}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N\mathscr{C}}
, which takes a map
F[d,k]\times\Delta[l]\to N\mathscr{C}
to
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(F[d,k]\times\Delta[l]\to N\mathscr{C})
. It then follows formally that the left Kan extension has a right adjoint
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
.
Now we observe that this adjunction is in fact
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched. However, this follows from the fact that
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
commutes with tensors (
3.34
) and
1.26
.
∎

Let us better understand the right adjoint
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
.
Let
G:\mathscr{C}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
be a
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor such that the functor

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{C}}(-,G):\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to\mathscr{S}

preserves level-wise equivalence (which holds in particular if
G
is projectively fibrant, see
3.37
).
and fix a map
p:F[d,n]\times\Delta[l]\to\mathscr{C}
that corresponds to the chain of morphisms
c_{0}\to...\to c_{n}
in
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}_{l}
. Then the space of fibers of
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G
over
p
is equivalent to the space
G(c_{0})[d]
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}(F[d,n]\times\Delta[l],s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))}(s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p),G)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))}(F[d]\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},-),G)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Map}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}(F[d],G(c_{0}))

\displaystyle\cong G(c_{0})[d]

Having constructed two adjunctions
(s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}},s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}})
and
(s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}},s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}})
we move on to show they are Quillen adjunctions. This requires us understanding the relevant model structures first!

Proposition 3.37
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. Then the category of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functors
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
(
1.16
) has two combinatorial proper
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched model structures, the projective model structure and the injective model structure given as follows.

•

A natural transformation
\alpha:F\to G
in both model structures is a weak equivalence if for all objects
c
the map of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces
\alpha_{c}:F(c)\to G(c)
is an injective equivalence.

•

A natural transformation
\alpha:F\to G
is a projective fibration if for all objects
c
the map of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces
\alpha_{c}:F(c)\to G(c)
is an injective fibration.

•

A natural transformation
\alpha:F\to G
is an injective cofibration if for all objects
c
the map of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces
\alpha_{c}:F(c)\to G(c)
is an injective cofibration.

Moreover, the generating (trivial) cofibrations of the projective model structure are of the form
\mathrm{id}\times\{\alpha\}:\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-)\times\{F\}\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-)\times\{G\}
, where
\alpha:F\to G
is (trivial) cofibration in
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
. Finally, the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{inj}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

gives us a
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Quillen equivalence. Here the left hand side has the projective model structure and the right hand side the injective model structure.

Proof.

The fact that this model structure exists and is combinatorial is a direct application of
[
Mos19
, Theorem 4.4]
with
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{A}=s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
with the injective model structure enriched over itself and
D=\mathscr{C}
. Indeed, it is evident that
-\times X:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
preserves (trivial) cofibrations. The fact that the model structure is proper follows from the fact that the injective model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
is proper and that each projective (injective) (co)fibration is a level-wise cofibration combined with the fact that pullbacks and pushouts are evaluated level-wise. The fact that it is enriched over
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
follow from the fact that the injective model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
is enriched over itself and
[
Mos19
, Theorem 5.4]
.

Finally, every (trivial) cofibration in the projective model structure is evidently a (trivial) cofibration in the injective model structure and so
\mathrm{id}:\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{inj}
is a Quillen left adjoint. Moreover, both sides have the same weak equivalences, hence the Quillen adjunction is a Quillen equivalence.
∎

Here are some comments regarding this model structure.

Lemma 3.38
.

A map
\alpha:F\to G
is a projective (or injective) equivalence if and only if for all
d
in
\mathcal{D}
and all objects
c
the map of spaces
\alpha_{c}[d]:F(c)[d]\to G(c)[d]
is a Kan equivalence.

Proof.

By definition
\alpha
is a projective equivalence if and only if
\alpha_{c}:F(c)\to G(c)
is an injective equivalence in
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
which, by
1.46
, is equivalent to
\alpha_{c}[d]:F(c)[d]\to G(c)[d]
being a Kan equivalence.
∎

Corollary 3.39
.

The projective and injective model structures on
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
are simplicial.

Proof.

Notice the simplicial enrichment is given by applying the projection map
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})\to\mathscr{S}
, which preserves cofibrations and trivial cofibrations. The result now follows from
3.38
.
∎

We can now study the model categorical aspects of the functor
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
.

Lemma 3.40
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. Then the functor
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}}
takes projective (trivial) cofibrations to
\mathcal{D}
-covariant (trivial) cofibrations. Moreover
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
reflects equivalences.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
takes generating (trivial) projective cofibrations to (trivial) cofibrations in the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure. By
3.37
, the generating (trivial) cofibrations are given by
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-)\times A\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-)\times B
where
A\to B
is a (trivial) cofibration of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. By
3.23
and
3.24
,
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
takes this map to
N\mathscr{C}_{c/}\times A\to N\mathscr{C}_{c/}\times B
, which is a (trivial) cofibration in the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure.

Now, let
\alpha:F\to G
be natural transformation such that
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha:s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F\to s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G
is a
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence. We want to prove
\alpha
is a level-wise equivalence. Notice for every object
d
in
\mathcal{D}
and
n
in
{\mathbbe{\Delta}}
, we have a bijection
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F[d,n]\cong s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F[d,0]\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}[d,0]}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}[d,n]
(
3.21
), which means the injectively fibrant replacement is already a
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration.

Taking the injectively fibrant replacement we get the following diagram where the second row is the fibrant replacement in the injective model structure
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rs\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rs\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rs\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha}

.

By definition
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha
is a covariant equivalence if and only if
Rs\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha
is an injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces, which, by
2.24
, is equivalent to
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(Rs\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha)
being an injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. By
3.21

\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha=\coprod_{c\in\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{C}}}\alpha_{c}:\coprod_{\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{C}}}F(c)\to\coprod_{\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{C}}}G(c)

and so
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(Rs\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha)
is a injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces over
\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{C}}
if and only if
\alpha_{c}:F(c)\to G(c)
is an injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces, which by definition means
\alpha
is an equivalence in the projective model structure (
3.37
).
∎

Lemma 3.41
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category.
Then the functor
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N\mathscr{C}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
takes (trivial)
\mathcal{D}
-covariant cofibrations to (trivial) injective cofibrations.

Proof.

Let
f:A\to B
be a map over
N\mathscr{C}
. Then, by
2.12
,
f
is a (trivial) cofibration if and only if for every
d
, the map of simplicial spaces
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}f:\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}A\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}B
is a (trivial) cofibration in the covariant model structure. Now, by
3.31
, we have

\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(p:Y\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})(c)=\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(Y[d]\times_{N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}}N\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathscr{C}_{/c}),

which preserves (trivial) cofibrations in the covariant model structure as it is left Quillen (
1.103
). Finally, by
3.37
, being a (trivial) cofibration in the injective model structure on
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
is determined level-wise.
∎

We now use these adjunctions to prove that we have the desired Quillen equivalences giving the appropriate generalization of
[
Ras17b
, Theorem 4.18]
.

Theorem 3.42
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category.
The two
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}})^{\mathcal{D}-cov}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{inj}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

are Quillen equivalences. Here the left hand
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
has the projective model structure,
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N\mathscr{C}}
has the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure over
N\mathscr{C}
and the right hand
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
has the injective model structure.

Proof.

First we observe that
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is left Quillen by
3.40
and
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is left Quillen by
3.41
showing both adjunctions are Quillen adjunctions.

Next we show that the composition functor
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\circ s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
is naturally equivalent to the identity functor
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
. This will then prove that the composition is a Quillen equivalence. As both functors
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
,
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
are left adjoints they commute with colimits and it suffices to show the desired equivalence for functors of the form
\{A\}\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c,-)
, where
A
is an
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space and
c
is an object in
\mathscr{C}
.

By
3.23
,
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\{A\}\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c,-)=\{A\}\times N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{c/}
, which by
2.31
is equivalent in
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure to the map
A\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
with constant value
c
. By
3.34
,
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(A\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})\simeq\{A\}\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c,-)
giving us the desired natural equivalence.

So, by
2
-out-of-
3
for Quillen equivalence, it suffices to prove the first is a Quillen equivalence. First of all, by
3.40
,
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
reflects equivalences. Hence, it suffices to check the derived counit map is an equivalence, which means we have to prove that for a given
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
L\to N\mathscr{C}
the map
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}L\to L
is a
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence over
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
.

We will in fact observe that the map is an injective equivalence. For an arbitrary
(d,k)
in
\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}
we have the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}L[d,k]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L[d,k]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}L[d,0]\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}[d,0]}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}[d,k]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L[d,0]\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}[d,0]}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}[d,k]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The left hand vertical map is a weak equivalence by assumption on
L
and the right hand vertical map is a bijection by construction (
3.21
). Hence the top map is an equivalence of spaces if and only if the bottom map is an equivalence. However, by
1.33
, the space
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}[d,0]
is discrete and so it suffices to prove that
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}L[d,0]\to L[d,0]
is an equivalence.
Now, every map
F[d,0]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
is necessarily constant with value an object of
\mathscr{C}
and so it suffices to fix a map
F[d,0]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
and prove we a natural equivalence

\mathrm{Map}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}(F[d,0],s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}L)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{N_{\mathcal{D}}/\mathscr{C}}(F[d,0],L).

We have the following chain of isomorphisms and equivalences:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}(F[d,0],s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}L)

\displaystyle\cong s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}L(c)[d]

\displaystyle\cong\underline{\mathrm{Nat}}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-)\times F[d],s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}L)

\displaystyle\cong\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}(s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(c,-)\times\{F[d]\}),L)

\displaystyle\cong\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{c/}\times F[d,0],L)

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}(F[d,0],L)

giving us the desired equivalence and finishing the proof.
∎

Notice the two functor categories in the statement of
3.42
have two different (even if Quillen equivalent) model structures and we would like to have a statement which involves only one model structure.

Theorem 3.43
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. Assume that
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
takes projective fibrations to injective fibration. Then, the two
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}})^{\mathcal{D}-cov}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

are Quillen equivalences. Here the two
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
have the projective model structure and
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
has the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure.

Proof.

We have the following diagram of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}})^{\mathcal{D}-cov}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{inj}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

where the middle
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
has the projective model structure and the right hand
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
has the injective model structure.
The composition of the two adjunctions is a Quillen equivalence by
3.37
and the right hand is a Quillen equivalence by
3.42
. Hence, by
2
-out-of-
3
it suffices to prove the left hand adjunction is a Quillen adjunction.

First we show
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
takes projective fibrations to
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations. By assumption
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
takes projective fibrations to injective fibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces, so it suffices to show the map satisfies the locality condition of
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration. By adjunction this is equivalent to
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(F[d,0]\to F[d,n])
being a projective equivalence in
\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}
. By
3.37
projective and injective equivalences coincide and so this follows from
3.41
.

We have hence proven that
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
takes projective fibrations between projectively fibrant functors to
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations between
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations, which are precisely the fibrations between fibrant objects in the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure (
2.14
). Moreover, it takes trivial projective fibrations to
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations, which are fiber-wise weak equivalences. By
2.24
, this means they are weak equivalences in the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure and so are trivial fibrations in the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure. Hence, by
[
JT07
, Proposition 7.15]
, it is a right Quillen functor.
∎

We will end this section with an analysis of the
universal
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
. Following
3.16
, the universal
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration is given by the projection functor
N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{D[0]/}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
(as defined in
3.26
). We now have the following analogous result.

Proposition 3.44
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. The functor

N_{\mathcal{D}}(-)^{*}\pi_{*}:\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}

given by pulling back
\pi_{*}
along
N_{\mathcal{D}}(-)
is precisely the functor
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
.

Proof.

The pullback functor and
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
are both colimit preserving so it suffices to compare them on representables
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c,-)\times\{A\}
where
c
is an object in
\mathscr{C}
and
A
is a
\mathcal{D}
-space. By
3.23
and
3.24
,
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c,-)\times\{A\}=N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{c/}\times A
. The same holds for
N_{\mathcal{D}}(-)^{*}\pi_{*}
by applying
3.16
.
∎

The next desired step would be to generalize it to a global equivalence not relying on
\mathscr{C}
, similar to
3.18
. However, that would require us to have a fiber-wise approach to model categories which quite challenging and not very developed. Instead we will postpone this discussion to
Section

3.3
, when we have translated the equivalence into the language of quasi-categories.

3.3.
Grothendieck Construction for
\mathcal{D}
-Simplicial Spaces with weakly Constant Objects

Up until now our Grothendieck construction has focused on functors over nerves of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories. In this subsection we want to generalize the result to more general
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces. For that we need the strictification results from
[
Ber07
]
. Bergner proves the existence of the following diagram of simplicial spaces

X\xleftarrow{c_{X}}CX\xrightarrow{u_{X}}RFCX

natural in the simplicial space
X
. Here the first map is a cofibrant replacement in a model structure for Segal categories given in
[
Ber07
, Theorem 7.1]
and is a weak equivalence in the complete Segal space model structure by
[
Ber07
, Theorem 6.3]
. The second map is the derived unit map of the Quillen equivalence given in
[
Ber07
, Theorem 8.6]
and is (again by
[
Ber07
, Theorem 6.3]
) an equivalence in the model structure for complete Segal spaces and the object
RFCX
is in fact the nerve (in the sense of
1.33
) of a simplicially enriched category.

Our goal is to prove that we can use these results by Bergner to construct a zigzag of level-wise complete Segal space equivalences between a large class of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces and nerves of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories. Before we proceed, let us simplify the notation and make it more consistent with with the notation we have been using up until now.

Notation 3.45
.

Let
\mathcal{C}:s\mathscr{S}\to s\mathscr{S}
denote the cofibrant replacement functor in model structure constructed by Bergner in
[
Ber07
, Theorem 7.1]
. Moreover, we use the notation
N\mathscr{C}_{-}:s\mathscr{S}\to s\mathscr{S}
for the functor that first applies the cofibrant replacement functor
\mathcal{C}
and then the adjunction
RFC
defined in
[
Ber07
, Theorem 8.6]
. Notice, these functors come with a natural zigzag of complete Segal space equivalences
X\leftarrow\mathcal{C}X\rightarrow N\mathscr{C}_{X}

We now proceed to generalize these construction to
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces.

Definition 3.46
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{D}}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
be the functor given by applying
\mathcal{C}
level-wise. More precisely, using the isomorphism
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},s\mathscr{S})\cong s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
we can define it as the post-composition functor

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{D}}=\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},\mathcal{C}):\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},s\mathscr{S})\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},s\mathscr{S}).

Similarly, define
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
as

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}=\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},N\mathscr{C}_{(-)}):\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},s\mathscr{S})\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{op},s\mathscr{S}).

Starting with an arbitrary
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}X
is level-wise the nerve of a simplicially enriched category, in the sense that for every object
d
in
\mathcal{D}

\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}X
is the nerve of a simplicially enriched category. This does not generally imply that the whole
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space is the nerve of a category enriched over
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
. For that we need the
underlying
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category
.

Definition 3.47
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object
t
.
Let
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{(-)}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
, be the functor that takes a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space
X
to
\mathbb{H}\mathcal{F}(X)
, where
\mathbb{H}
was defined in
1.74
.

We now have the following result regarding these definitions using
1.77
.

Lemma 3.48
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object.
Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects. Then there exists a zigzag natural in
X

X\overset{c_{X}}{\longleftarrow}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{D}}X\overset{u_{X}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}X\overset{r_{X}}{\longleftarrow}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X},

where
\mathscr{C}_{X}
is an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category and such that all morphisms in the zigzag are level-wise complete Segal space equivalences.

Remark 3.49
.

As already mentioned in
1.85
, we are not claiming (and it is not necessary for our purposes) that the object
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}
we constructed here is a fibrant object in a model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
. Indeed, according to
[
BR20
, Beginning of Section 8]
constructing model structures for Segal categories on
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces does not seem to be possible for arbitrary
\mathcal{D}
.

We can use this result to state the following valuable result.

Theorem 3.50
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object.
Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects. Then we have the following diagram of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Quillen equivalences.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}})^{\mathcal{D}-cov}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{inj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}X})^{\mathcal{D}-cov}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{D}}X})^{\mathcal{D}-cov}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{\mathcal{D}-cov}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(r_{X})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(r_{X})^{*}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u_{X})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u_{X})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})^{*}}

Proof.

The horizontal Quillen equivalences follow from
3.42
and the vertical Quillen equivalences from
2.18
combined with the fact that
r_{X},c_{X},u_{X}
are level-wise complete Segal space equivalences. ∎

This result has the following direct corollary that is worth stating explicitly and can be thought of as a
strictification of
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations
.

Corollary 3.51
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object.
Let
L\to X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration over a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space
X
with weakly constant objects. Then there exists an (up to equivalence) unique
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor
G:\mathscr{C}_{X}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
and a diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})^{*}L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{(u_{X})_{!}(c_{X})^{*}L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{D}}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq_{CSS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq_{CSS}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq_{CSS}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq_{inj}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq_{CSS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq_{CSS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq_{CSS}}

satisfying the following conditions:

•

The squares are homotopy pullback squares.

•

The horizontal morphism
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}F\to s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}F
is an injective equivalence.

•

The remaining three horizontal morphisms are all level-wise complete Segal space equivalences.

•

The vertical morphism
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}F\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}
is a
\mathcal{D}
-left morphism.

•

The remaining four vertical morphisms are
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations.

We can use this result to better understand the relation between values of and the fibers. As is often the case we first need a technical lemma regarding simplicial spaces.

Lemma 3.52
.

Let
X
be an arbitrary simplicial space. Let
x,y:F[0]\to X
be two maps. Let
X\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}\hookrightarrow\hat{X}
be a choice of fibrant replacement in the Segal space model structure (
\tilde{X}
) followed by a complete Segal space fibrant replacement
\hat{X}
. Then the following statements are equivalent.

(1)

The objects
x,y
in
\tilde{X}
are equivalent in the Segal space.

(2)

The points
x,y
in
\hat{X}
are in the same path component of
\hat{X}_{0}
.

(3)

The objects
x,y
in the homotopy category
\mathrm{Ho}\tilde{X}
are isomorphic.

Proof.

The equivalence
(1)\Leftrightarrow(3)
follows from the definition of homotopy equivalences in Segal spaces
[
Rez01
, Subsection 5.5]
and the equivalence
(1)\Leftrightarrow(2)
from the implication of the completeness condition
[
Rez01
, Proposition 6.4 (4)]
.
∎

Definition 3.53
.

Let
X
be a simplicial space. Define an equivalence relation on
X_{00}
as follows:
x\sim y
if and only if it satisfies one of the three equivalent conditions in
3.52
.

Lemma 3.54
.

Let
X\simeq Y
be two simplicial spaces that are equivalent in the complete Segal space model structure. Then we have a bijection
X_{00}/_{\sim}\cong Y_{00}/_{\sim}
, where
\sim
is the equivalence relation defined in
3.53
.

Proof.

Let
X\to\hat{X}
be a choice of fibrant replacement in the complete Segal space model structure, which, by
[
Rez01
, Theorem 7.2]
, is obtained by applying the small object argument to maps of the form

•

(\partial F[n]\to F[n])\square(\Lambda[l]_{i}\to\Delta[l])
,

•

(G[n]\to F[n])\square(\partial\Delta[l]\to\Delta[l])
,

•

(F[0]\to E[1])\square(\partial\Delta[l]\to\Delta[l])
.

Then the map
X_{0}\to\hat{X}
is surjective on path-components as all three classes of maps in the list above have this property. This means the map
X_{00}/_{\sim}\to\pi_{0}(\hat{X}_{0})
is surjective. On the other hand, by
3.52
,
x\sim y
in
X_{00}
if and only if
[x]=[y]
in
\pi_{0}(\hat{X}_{0})
, which implies that the map
X_{00}/_{\sim}\to\pi_{0}(\hat{X}_{0})
is injective as well. Hence, we have a bijection
X_{00}/_{\sim}\cong\pi_{0}(\hat{X}_{0})
.

Finally, recall that two equivalent complete Segal spaces are necessarily Reedy equivalent, so the result follows from the following chain of bijections

X_{00}/_{\sim}\cong\pi_{0}(\hat{X}_{0})\cong\pi_{0}(\hat{Y}_{0})\cong Y_{00}/_{\sim},

where
\hat{X}
and
\hat{Y}
are the fibrant replacement of
X
and
Y
in the complete Segal space model structure.
∎

The lemma implies that there is a bijection between equivalence classes of objects of
X
and
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}
. Let us denote an object in
X
and its corresponding object in
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}
both by
x
. Then we have the following result.

Corollary 3.55
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object and let
X
have weakly constant objects. Let
L_{x}\to X
be the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant fibrant replacement of the map
\{x\}:F[t,0]\to X
. Then corresponding functor given via
3.51
is the corepresentable functor
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(x,-):\mathscr{C}_{X}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
. In particular for every
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
L
with corresponding functor
F
we have an equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}L\simeq F(x)
.

Proof.

By assumption the map
F[t,0]\to L_{x}
is a
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence over
X
, which, by
2.12
, is equivalent to
F[0]\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}L_{x}
being a covariant equivalence over
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}X
for all objects
d
in
\mathcal{D}
. By
[
Ras17b
, Theorem 3.55]
, this implies that
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}L_{x}
has an initial object for all object
d
in
\mathcal{D}
. Now, by
3.51
, we have a complete Segal space equivalence
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}L_{x}\simeq\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G
for all objects
d
in
\mathcal{D}
, which means that
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{d}s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G
also has a terminal object. Again, by
[
Ras17b
, Theorem 3.55]
, this means it is a representable left fibration and in particular we have an injective equivalence
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G\simeq(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X})_{x/}
, which, by
3.23
, implies that
G\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(x,-)
.

Now, the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Quillen equivalences in
3.42
imply that for given left fibrations
L,L^{\prime}
over
X
with corresponding functors
G,G^{\prime}
, we have an equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces

(3.56)

\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/X}(L,L^{\prime})\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Nat}}(G,G^{\prime}).

So, fix an object
x
in
X
and let
\{x\}:F[t,0]\to X
be the constant map. Then for every
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
L\to X
we have equivalences
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces

\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}L\cong\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/X}(F[t,0],L)\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/X}(L_{x},L)\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}}(\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(x,-),G)\cong G(x),

where the middle equivalence follows from
3.56
and the second bijection from the enriched Yoneda lemma (
1.27
),
giving us the desired equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.
∎

Example 3.57
.

Let
W
be a level-wise Segal space with weakly constant objects. Then
W^{op}\times W
is also a level-wise Segal space with weakly constant objects (where
W^{op}
was defined in
1.34
). By
3.51
, the
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
\mathrm{Tw}(W)\to W^{op}\times W
(
2.35
) corresponds to an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-functor
(\mathscr{C}_{W})^{op}\times\mathscr{C}_{W}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
. Here we used the fact that the category corresponding to the
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space
W^{op}
is
(\mathscr{C}_{W})^{op}
, by
1.35
. Notice, as described in
1.110
, the fiber of
\mathrm{Tw}(W)
over a point
x,y
in
W^{op}\times W
is the mapping space
\mathrm{map}_{W}(x,y)
(
1.69
). Hence, using the same argument used in
3.55
, the functor is the mapping object functor

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{C}_{W}}(-,-):(\mathscr{C}_{W})^{op}\times\mathscr{C}_{W}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}).

Example 3.58
.

We can use the same arguments as in
3.57
and apply them to
2.37
and
2.36
. Hence, we deduce that for every level-wise Segal space
W
with weakly constant objects there are two functors

W_{-/}:\mathscr{C}_{W}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}),

W_{/-}:(\mathscr{C}_{W})^{op}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}),

where the value of
W_{-/}
at an object
x
is equivalent to the level-wise under-Segal space
W_{x/}
and
W_{/-}
the level-wise over Segal space
W_{/x}
.

Unfortunately the Quillen equivalences cannot be composed to a direct Quillen equivalence between the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X}
and the projective model structure on
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
.

We end this section by observing that we can construct an equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched quasi-categories
, as defined in
[
GH15
]
, out of the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Quillen equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-model structures, which do in fact give us direct equivalences of quasi-categories, rather than just a zigzag. Concretely, in
[
Hau15
]
Haugseng proves an equivalence between enriched model categories and their Quillen equivalences and enriched quasi-categories, which we can apply to our enriched Quillen equivalences. Before we can proceed we need the following notational convention from
[
Hau15
]
.

Notation 3.59
.

For a given simplicial model category
\mathcal{M}
, denote the underlying quasi-category by
\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]
. We use the same notation for the underlying enriched quasi-category of an enriched model category. Moreover for a given Cartesian closed quasi-category
\mathscr{C}
, denote the quasi-category of quasi-categories by
\mathscr{Q}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}
and the quasi-category of
\mathscr{C}
-enriched quasi-categories by
\mathscr{Q}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{\mathscr{C}}
.

Following
3.59
, let
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]
denote the underlying quasi-category of the Cartesian model injective structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
and notice it is a Cartesian closed quasi-category. For a strictly
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category
\mathscr{C}
, we again use
\mathscr{C}
to denote the corresponding
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched quasi-category. Now, again by
3.59
, the underlying quasi-category of the functor category is denoted
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]
. Notice, by
[
Hau15
, Theorem 1.1]
, there is an equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]
-enriched quasi-categories

(3.60)

\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]\simeq\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{Q}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]),

meaning there is an equivalence between the underlying quasi-category of the enriched projective model structure and the enriched functor quasi-category.

We will make one exception to
3.59
. For an arbitrary
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space the bifibrant objects in the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X}
are precisely the
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations over
X
. Hence, the underlying
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched quasi-category has objects
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations and we denote it by
\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}/X}
. Now applying
[
Hau15
, Theorem 1.1]
to
3.50
gives us the following result.

Corollary 3.61
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object.
Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects.
There is an equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched quasi-categories
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{Q}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]}}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{Q}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]}}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{D}}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}/X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(r_{X})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(r_{X})^{*}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u_{X})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u_{X})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})^{*}}

Unlike in the in the context of model categories in the context of quasi-categories in any adjoint equivalence a left adjoint is also a right adjoint
[
RV21
, Proposition 2.1.12]
. Hence, we can in fact compose the various equivalences to get the following final result.

Corollary 3.62
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object.
Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects.
There is an equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched quasi-categories
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{Q}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]}}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}/X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

.

Having done an analysis of the quasi-category we move on to generalize to a global equivalence. This will require some further quasi-categorical techniques and notation. Let
\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
be the underlying quasi-category of the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure (
1.83
). The object
N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
is by construction an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal space, but might not satisfy the completeness condition. Hence, let

(3.63)

\widehat{\pi_{*}}:\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{D[0]/}}\to\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}

be the completion of
\pi_{*}:N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{D[0]/}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
in the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure (
1.83
) and notice we have the following result.

Lemma 3.64
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a small
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. The functor

N_{\mathcal{D}}(-)^{*}\widehat{\pi}_{*}:\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}])\to\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}/X}

given by pulling back
N_{\mathcal{D}}
along
\widehat{\pi}_{*}
is equivalent to the functor
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
.

Proof.

The completion functor is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence (
1.83
) and so the argument follows from the same proof as in
3.44
.
∎

Let
\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{(all)}}_{\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}}\to\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
be the right fibration of quasi-categories representing the functor
((\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})})_{/-})^{grpd}
, where
(-)^{grpd}
is the underlying
\infty
-groupoid. See
[
GK17
, Subsection 3.3]
or
[
Ras21c
, Section 2]
for a more detailed discussion. Moreover, let
\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{(all)}}_{\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}}
be the full sub quasi-category consisting of left fibrations, which is also a right fibration over
\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
. By the Yoneda lemma
[
RV21
, Theorem 5.7.1]
, we have a map of quasi-categories
(\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})})_{/\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}}\to\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}
over
\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
