(\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}})_{/\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}}}\to\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
over
\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}}
, that takes a map
F:\mathscr{C}\to\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}}
to the pullback
F^{*}\widehat{\pi_{*}}
. We now have the following analogous result.

Corollary 4.59
.

We have the following diagram of right fibrations over the inclusion
\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}}\hookrightarrow\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}})_{/\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})})_{/\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the horizontal maps are equivalences and the vertical maps are inclusions.

Remark 4.60
.

4.59
implies that
\widehat{\pi_{*}}:\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}_{D[0]/}}\to\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}}
is a
universal
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
. More interestingly, building on
3.66
, the inclusions of right fibrations in
4.59
imply that for every
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
p:L\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
, there is a (homotopically unique) pullback of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}^{S}_{D[0]/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}_{D[0]/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}^{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

.

that factors through
N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}
if and only if
p
is
S
-localized.

Again,
[
Ras21c
, Proposition 2.4]
implies the following result regarding univalence.

Corollary 4.61
.

The universal
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
\widehat{\pi_{*}}:\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}_{D[0]/}\to\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
is univalent.

Notice that we could have deduced
4.61
also by using
3.67
with
[
Ras21c
, Proposition 2.5]
.

4.3.
Further Properties of the
S
-Localized
\mathcal{D}
-Left Fibrations

We move on to understand the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure, in particular its equivalences.

Theorem 4.62
.

Let
L
and
M
be two
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations over
X
.
Let
g:L\to M
be a map over
X
. Then the following are equivalent.

(1)

g:L\to M
is an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence.

(2)

g:L\to M
is an injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces.

(3)

\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(g):\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(L)\to\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(M)
is an injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.

(4)

For every
\{x\}:F[d,0]\to X
, the map
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(L)\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(M)
is an injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces.

(5)

For every
\{x\}:F[d,0]\to X
, the map
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}(L)\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}(M)
is a
\mathcal{D}
-diagonal equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces.

Proof.

The first two conditions are equivalent as
L\to X
,
M\to X
are fibrant objects in the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure and
4.17
. The other condition are all equivalent to the second condition by
2.24
and the fact that
L\to X,M\to X
are
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations.
∎

Proposition 4.63
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
have a terminal object
t
, let
S
be a set of cofibrations and assume
X
has weakly constant objects.
Let
p:L\to X
,
q:M\to X
be
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations (not necessarily localized) and let
f:L\to M
be a map over
X
.
Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

f
is an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence.

(2)

For every object
\{x\}:F[t,0]\to X
, the induced map on fibers

\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}L\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}M

is a diagonal
S
-localized injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces.

(3)

For every object
\{x\}:F[t,0]\to X
, the induced map on fibers

\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}L)\to\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}M)

is an
S
-localized injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.

Proof.

(1)
\Leftrightarrow
(2)

The map
L\to M
is level-wise CSS equivalent (via a zigzag of equivalences by
3.51
) to a map of the form
s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}G\to s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}H
, where
G:\mathscr{C}\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
is an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor with
G(c)\simeq\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}L
(
3.55
) and the same for
H
. Hence, by the Quillen equivalence in
3.50
,
L\to M
is an equivalence in the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure if and only if
G\to H
is a level-wise equivalence in the
S
-localized injective model structure, which is precisely the statement that
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}L\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}M
is a diagonal
S
-localized injective equivalence.

(2)
\Leftrightarrow
(3)

This follows from the fact that
L\to X
is a
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration and so

\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}(L)\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}(L)
is an injective equivalence (
2.15
).
∎

Theorem 4.64
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
have a terminal object
t
, let
S
be a set of cofibrations and assume
X
has a weakly constant objects.
A map
g:Y\to Z
of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces over
X
is an equivalence in the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure if and only if for each map
\{x\}:F[t,0]\to X
, the induced map

Y\underset{X}{\times}R_{x}\to Z\underset{X}{\times}R_{x}

is an equivalence in the diagonal
S
-localized injective model structure.
Here
R_{x}
is a choice of
\mathcal{D}
-right fibrant replacement of the map
\{x\}
.

Proof.

Let
\hat{g}:\hat{Y}\to\hat{Z}
be a fibrant replacement of
g
in the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure (note: not localized).
Moreover, let
\{x\}:F[t,0]\to X
be a vertex in
X
.
This gives us the following zigzag of maps:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{Y}\underset{X}{\times}F[t,0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{Z}\underset{X}{\times}F[t,0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{Y}\underset{X}{\times}R_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{Z}\underset{X}{\times}R_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\underset{X}{\times}R_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\underset{X}{\times}R_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

.

According to
2.23
the top vertical maps are
\mathcal{D}
-contravariant equivalences and the bottom vertical maps are
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalences. By
2.15
both of these are
\mathcal{D}
-diagonal equivalences, which are always diagonal localized injective equivalences (
4.14
). Thus the top map is a diagonal localized injective equivalence if and only if the bottom map is one, but, by
4.63
, this is equivalent to
Y\to Z
being a localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence over
X
.
∎

We can use this result to prove the following invariance property, giving a result similar to
4.24
with different assumptions.

Theorem 4.65
.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}
be two small categories with terminal objects
t_{1},t_{2}
. Moreover, let
S_{1}
(
S_{2}
) be a set of monomorphisms in
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
(
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{2})
) with contractible codomain. Assume there exists a Quillen adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{1})^{inj_{S_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{2})^{inj_{S_{2}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}

.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}_{1}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects. Then this induces a Quillen adjunction (using
4.23
).
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{1}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{\mathcal{D}_{1}-cov_{S_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{2}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/sLX})^{\mathcal{D}_{2}-cov_{S_{2}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sL}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sR}

,

for all
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
-simplicial spaces
X
which is simplicial or a Quillen equivalence if
(L,R)
is one.

Proof.

First let us assume
(L,R)
is a Quillen adjunction. Using again the fact that
F[d,n]\cong F[d,0]\times D[n]
(
1.40
) and the same argument as in the proof of
4.24
(and in particular right below
4.27
) it follows that
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{1}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{\mathcal{D}_{1}-cov}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{2}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/sLX})^{\mathcal{D}_{2}-cov}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sL}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sR}

is a Quillen adjunction from the
\mathcal{D}_{1}
-covariant model structure to the
\mathcal{D}_{2}
-covariant model structure. We want to prove it remains a Quillen adjunction of localized model structures. By
4.33
in order to obtain localized covariant model structures it suffices to localize with respect to maps of the form

\{\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A)\to\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(B)\to D[0]\to X:A\to B\in S_{1}\},

\{\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A)\to\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(B)\to D[0]\to sLX:A\to B\in S_{2}\}.

By
4.9
it suffices to prove that
sL(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A)\to\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(B)\to D[0]\to X)=\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(L(A))\to\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(L(B))\to D[0]\to sLX
is an
S_{2}
-localized
\mathcal{D}_{2}
-covariant equivalence. However, by assumption
(L,R)
is a Quillen adjunction between
S_{1}
-localized and the
S_{2}
-localized injective model structures and so the result again follows from
4.28
. This proves the adjunction is a Quillen adjunction, which is simplicial if
(L,R)
is.

Now, we assume that
(L,R)
is a Quillen equivalence. Using the zigzag of Quillen equivalences given in
4.54
we can assume that
X=N_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathscr{C}_{1}
, where
\mathscr{C}_{1}
is an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
-enriched category. In that case
sL(X)=N_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\mathscr{C}_{2}
, where
\mathscr{C}_{2}
has the same objects as
\mathscr{C}_{1}
and for two objects
c,d
in
\mathscr{C}_{1}
,
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}_{2}}(c,d)\simeq L\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}_{1}}(c,d)
(using the fact that the left Quillen equivalence
L
commutes with products up to equivalence).

We want to prove the Quillen adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{1}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathscr{C}_{1}})^{\mathcal{D}_{1}-cov}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}_{2}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\mathscr{C}_{2}})^{\mathcal{D}_{2}-cov}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sL}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sR}

,

is a Quillen equivalence. We will prove that the right adjoint reflects equivalences between fibrant objects and the derived unit map is an equivalence of
S_{1}
-localized
\mathcal{D}_{1}
-left fibrations.

Let
Y\to N_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathscr{C}_{1}
be an
S_{1}
-localized
\mathcal{D}_{1}
-left fibration. Let
\widehat{sL(Y)}\to N_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\mathscr{C}_{2}
be the fibrant replacement of
sL(Y)\to N_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\mathscr{C}_{2}
in the
S_{2}
-localized
\mathcal{D}_{2}
-covariant model structure and notice by
4.63
for every object
c
in
\mathscr{C}_{2}
, the fiber
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}sL(Y)\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}\widehat{sL(Y)}
is a fibrant replacement in the
S_{2}
-localized injective model structure on
\mathcal{D}_{2}
-spaces. Applying
sR
we get the derived unit map
sL(Y)\to sR\widehat{sL(Y)}
, which over an object
c
restricts to the map
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}Y\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}sR\widehat{sL(Y)}
. As
sL,sR
are defined level-wise and the fiber of a
\mathcal{D}_{1}
-left fibration is constant (
2.15
) we have the injective equivalence
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}sR\widehat{sL(Y)}\simeq R\widehat{L\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}Y}
, meaning the derived unit map
sL(Y)\to sR\widehat{sL(Y)}
restricts over the object
c
to the derived unit map of the
cD
-space
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}Y
. By the assumption that
(L,R)
is a Quillen equivalence, this map is an
S_{1}
-localized equivalence of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
-spaces, which, by
4.62
, implies that the derived unit map of
L
is an
S_{1}
-localized
\mathcal{D}_{1}
-covariant equivalence.

Now, let
Y\to Z
be a map of
S_{2}
-localized
\mathcal{D}_{2}
-left fibrations over
N_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\mathscr{C}_{2}
, such that
sR(Y)\to sR(Z)
is an
S_{1}
-localized
\mathcal{D}_{2}
-equivalence over
N_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathscr{C}_{1}
. By
4.62
, the assumption implies that
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}sR(Y)\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}sR(Z)
is an injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
-spaces (as both
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}sR(Y)
,
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}sR(Z)
are fibrant in the
S_{1}
-localized injective model structure). However, this map is injectively equivalent to
R(\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}(Y))\to R(\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}(Z))
and
R
reflects equivalences, which means that
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}(Y)\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{(}Z)
is an
S_{2}
-localized injective equivalence, which implies that
Y\to Z
is an
S_{2}
-localized
\mathcal{D}_{2}
-covariant equivalence (
4.62
).
∎

We want to generalize
4.19
to
S
-localized equivalences, however, this requires a technical detour. First we need the following definition.

Definition 4.66
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
have a terminal object
t
. A functor
F:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence if it satisfies the following two conditions.

•

Fully faithful:
For all objects
c,c^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{C}
, the map of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces

\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c,c^{\prime})\to\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(Fc,Fc^{\prime})

is an
S
-localized injective equivalence.

•

Essentially Surjective:
The map of simplicially enriched categories
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{t}F:\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{t}\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}_{t}\mathscr{D}
induces a bijection on equivalence classes of objects
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{C}})\to\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathscr{D}})
.

We have following evident lemma with regard to
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalences and Dwyer-Kan equivalences (
1.47
).

Lemma 4.67
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a category with terminal object and
S
a set of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. Every Dwyer-Kan equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories
F:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence. Moreover, the opposite holds if
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{D}
are enriched over
S
-local
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.

We want to be able to relate
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalences between general categories with Dwyer-Kan equivalences. For that we first need the following condition on mapping spaces.

Definition 4.68
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a category with terminal objects and let
S
be a set of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. Fix a functorial fibrant replacement
R:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
in the
S
-localized injective model structure. An
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal category (
1.68
) has
Cartesian mapping spaces
if for all objects
c,c^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
-space
A
the map
A\times\mathrm{map}(c,c^{\prime})\to A\times R\mathrm{map}(c,c^{\prime})
is an
S
-localized injective equivalence. Moreover, an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category has Cartesian mapping spaces if
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
has Cartesian mapping spaces.

Notation 4.69
.

We denote the full sub-category of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces consisting of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal categories with Cartesian mapping spaces by
\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}^{cart}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
.

Lemma 4.70
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a category with terminal objects and let
S
be a geometrically contractible set of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. Let
R:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
be a functorial fibrant replacement in the
S
-localized injective model structure and define
sR:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
as applying
R
level-wise (
4.23
). Then
sR
restricts to a functor (using
4.69
)

sR:\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}^{cart}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}\to\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}^{cart}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}

that takes a Segal category to one with the same objects.

Proof.

Fix an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal category with Cartesian mapping spaces
X
. As
S
consists of geometric equivalence,
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(sR(X))=R(\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(X))=\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(X)=\mathrm{Obj}_{X}
, as
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(X)
is discrete and hence fibrant (
4.6
). Next, by definition of a Segal category we have the injective equivalence
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{k}(X)\simeq\coprod_{x_{0},...,x_{k}}\mathrm{map}(x_{0},x_{1})\times...\times\mathrm{map}(x_{k-1},x_{k})
and so we get the injective equivalence

(4.71)

R\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{k}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle R(\coprod_{x_{0},...,x_{k}}\mathrm{map}(x_{0},x_{1})\times...\times\mathrm{map}(x_{k-1},x_{k}))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\coprod_{x_{0},...,x_{k}}R(\mathrm{map}(x_{0},x_{1})\times...\times\mathrm{map}(x_{k-1},x_{k}))
,

where in the last step we used the fact that fibrant replacements preserve coproducts (
4.7
).
Now, for given mapping spaces
\mathrm{map}_{X}(c,c^{\prime})
,
\mathrm{map}_{X}(d,d^{\prime})
we can use the fact that
X
has Cartesian mapping spaces (
4.68
) twice to get
S
-localized injective equivalences

\mathrm{map}_{X}(c,c^{\prime})\times\mathrm{map}_{X}(d,d^{\prime})\to R\mathrm{map}_{X}(c,c^{\prime})\times\mathrm{map}_{X}(d,d^{\prime})\to R\mathrm{map}_{X}(c,c^{\prime})\times R\mathrm{map}_{X}(d,d^{\prime}),

which gives us an injective equivalence

R\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{X}(c,c^{\prime})\times R\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{X}(d,d^{\prime})\simeq R(\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{X}(c,c^{\prime})\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{X}(d,d^{\prime}))

as both sides are fibrant in the
S
-localized injective model structure. Combining this equivalence with
4.71
gives us the desired injective equivalences

\displaystyle R\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{k}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq\coprod_{x_{0},...,x_{k}}R(\mathrm{map}(x_{0},x_{1})\times...\times\mathrm{map}(x_{k-1},x_{k}))

\displaystyle\simeq\coprod_{x_{0},...,x_{k}}R\mathrm{map}(x_{0},x_{1})\times...\times R\mathrm{map}(x_{k-1},x_{k})

\displaystyle\simeq R\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{1}(X)\times_{\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(X)}...\times_{\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(X)}R\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{1}(X)

proving that
sRX
is a Segal category with the same set of objects.
∎

Lemma 4.72
.

There exists a functor

\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}:\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}\to\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{\Delta}

from Segal categories to simplicially enriched categories, which takes a Segal category
X
to a simplicially enriched category
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(X)
with the following properties:

•

\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(X)
has the same objects as
X
.

•

There is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(X)\to\mathscr{C}_{X}
(
3.45
).

•

If
X=N\mathscr{C}
, the nerve of a simplicially enriched category, then we have the diagram
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(N\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{C}_{N\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{C}
, such that the composition map is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence that is the identity on objects.

Proof.

Let
X
be a Segal category and let
X[0]
be the discrete simplicial space given by the set
X[0]
and notice there is an evident inclusion map
X[0]\to X
. Notice, the functor
N\mathscr{C}_{(-)}
defined in
3.45
takes
X[0]=\coprod_{\mathrm{Obj}_{X}}F[0]
to the discrete category
\coprod_{\mathrm{Obj}_{X}}[0]
(as it is a left adjoint and preserves coproducts), which we can simply denote as the discrete category
\mathrm{Obj}_{X}
.

Now applying
N\mathscr{C}_{(-)}
to the map
X[0]\to X
gives us the functor
\mathrm{Obj}_{X}\to\mathscr{C}_{X}
. Let
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(X)
be the full subcategory of
\mathscr{C}_{X}
with objects in
\mathrm{Obj}_{X}
given via the functor
\mathrm{Obj}_{X}\to\mathscr{C}_{X}
. Notice, this inclusion via the full subcategory is in fact essentially surjective and hence a Dwyer-Kan equivalence. Indeed, by
[
Ber07
, Theorem 7.1]
, we have a zigzag of Dwyer-Kan equivalences of Segal categories

X\leftarrow\mathcal{C}(X)\to N\mathscr{C}_{X}

and notice the Segal category
N\mathscr{C}_{X}
and the simplicially enriched category
\mathscr{C}_{X}
have the same objects.

Now, if
X=N\mathscr{C}
, then by
[
Ber07
, Theorem 8.6]
we have a derived counit map
\mathscr{C}_{N\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{C}
and so, combining with the previous step, we get the desired map
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{C}_{N\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{C}
, which is, by construction of
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(\mathscr{C})
, the identity on objects.
∎

Lemma 4.73
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a category with terminal objects and let
S
be a geometrically contractible set of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. There exists a functor
R:\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}\to\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}
, such that for all
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories
\mathscr{C}
with Cartesian mapping spaces,
R\mathscr{C}
has the same objects as
\mathscr{C}
and
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{R\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
is the fibrant replacement of
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
in the
S
-localized injective model structure. Moreover, there is a zigzag of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories

\mathscr{C}\overset{\simeq}{\leftarrow}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{C})\rightarrow R\mathscr{C}

where both maps are identity on objects and the left hand map is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence.

Proof.

Let
R
be defined as the composition of the following three functors

\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}\xrightarrow{\ N_{\mathcal{D}}\ }s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})\xrightarrow{\ sR\ }s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})\xrightarrow{\ s\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}\ }\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})},

where
sR
is defined in
4.70
and
s\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}
applies the functor
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}
(
4.72
) level-wise (
4.23
). By
4.70
, we have a map of Segal categories
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}\to sRN_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
, which is the identity on objects and so applying
s\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}
, we get

s\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})\to R\mathscr{C}
. Using
4.72
, we can extend this diagram to the desired zigzag

\mathscr{C}\overset{\simeq}{\leftarrow}s\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})\to R\mathscr{C}

where the left hand arrow is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence, both maps are identity on objects and right hand map is given as fibrant replacement and so the desired result follows with
\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{C})=s\mathscr{S}\mathrm{tr}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})
.
∎

We would like to say that a functor of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories
\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence (
4.66
) if and only if
R\mathscr{C}\to R\mathscr{D}
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence. However, for this conclusion we need one more assumptions.

Definition 4.74
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category. We say
\mathscr{C}
has
S
-stable objects
if the map
\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{C})\to R\mathscr{C}
constructed in
4.73
induces a bijection of equivalence classes of objects
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{C})\cong\pi_{0}(\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{C}))\cong\pi_{0}(R\mathscr{C})
.

Lemma 4.75
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a category with terminal objects and let
S
be a geometrically contractible set of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.
Then a functor
F:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories with Cartesian mapping spaces is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence if and only if
RF:R\mathscr{C}\to R\mathscr{D}
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence.

Proof.

The natural zigzag in
4.73
gives us a diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\mathscr{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\mathscr{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\mathscr{D}}}

.

The top vertical maps are Dwyer-Kan equivalences and so give us an injective equivalence of mapping spaces and and a bijection of equivalence classes of objects. The bottom vertical maps are
S
-localized equivalences of mapping spaces (by
4.73
) and a bijection of equivalence classes of objects, by assumption (
4.74
). Hence, the result follows by
2
-out-of-
3
.
∎

We now have the necessary background results and need one final definition.

Definition 4.76
.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects. Then
X
has
Cartesian mapping spaces
with
S
-stable objects
if the corresponding category
\mathscr{C}_{X}
(
3.48
) has Cartesian mapping spaces (
4.68
) and has
S
-stable objects (
4.74
).

Definition 4.77
.

A map of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with weakly constant objects is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence if the map
\mathscr{C}_{X}\to\mathscr{C}_{Y}
given via
3.48
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories (
4.66
).

Remark 4.78
.

Notice,
4.76
implies that having Cartesian mapping spaces, having
S
-stable objects and being an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence is invariant under level-wise complete Segal space equivalences of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with weakly constant objects.

Under the appropriate conditions we can relate
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalences to
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalences (
4.42
).

Lemma 4.79
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object
t
. Let
X,Y
be
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with weakly constant objects, Cartesian mapping spaces and
S
-stable objects. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

f:X\to Y
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence.

(2)

R\mathscr{C}_{f}:R\mathscr{C}_{X}\to R\mathscr{C}_{Y}
constructed in
4.73
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence.

(3)

f:X\to Y
is an
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalence.

Proof.

The equivalence of the first two items is the content of
4.77
and
4.75
. Now notice
f
is an
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalence if and only if
N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{f}
is one. Indeed, all maps involved in the zigzag between
f
and
N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{f}
are either level-wise complete Segal space equivalences or
S
-localized equivalences, both are which are
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalences.

Now, notice that the fibrant replacement of
N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{f}
in the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure
\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{f}}
is already the fibrant replacement in the
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure (by
4.40
and
4.38
). By definition
N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{f}
is an
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalence if and only if
\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{f}}
is an injective equivalence. However, by
1.80
,
N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{f}
and
\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{f}}
are also Dwyer-Kan equivalent and so this is equivalent to
R\mathscr{C}_{f}
being a Dwyer-Kan equivalence.
∎

We are finally in a position to improve upon
4.19
as promised in
4.20
.

Theorem 4.80
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
have a terminal object
t
and
X,Y
weakly constant objects. Let
S
be geometrically contractible set of cofibration of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. Let
g:X\to Y
be a map of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with Cartesian mapping
\mathcal{D}
-spaces and
S
-stable objects. Then the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/Y})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen adjunction, which is a Quillen equivalence whenever
g
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence (
4.77
) or
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalence (
4.42
). Here both sides have the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure.

Proof.

It is evidently a Quillen adjunction as
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations are stable under pullback and
g_{!}
preserves cofibrations and
[
Lur09a
, Corollary A.3.7.2]
. Now, let us assume
g
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence, which, by
4.79
, is equivalent to being an
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalence. We want to prove
(g_{!},g^{*})
is a Quillen equivalence. By
3.48
,
g
is level-wise complete Segal space equivalent to a map of nerves
N_{\mathcal{D}}G:N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{D}
and so, by
4.19
and
2
-out-of-
3
for Quillen equivalences,
(g_{!},g^{*})
is a Quillen equivalence if and only if
(N_{\mathcal{D}}G_{!},N_{\mathcal{D}}G^{*})
is a Quillen equivalence.

Now, using
4.75
we get the following diagram of Quillen adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{D}})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{C})})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{D})})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{D}})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}G_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{\mathscr{C}})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}G^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{\mathscr{D}})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}G_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{\mathscr{C}})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{\mathscr{C}})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}G^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{\mathscr{D}})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{\mathscr{D}})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}RG_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{\mathscr{C}})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}RG^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{\mathscr{D}})^{*}}

By
4.75

R\mathscr{C}\to R\mathscr{D}
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence and so the bottom adjunction is a Quillen equivalence by
1.80
and
4.19
. Moreover, applying the same result to
4.73
implies that the top vertical Quillen adjunctions are Quillen equivalences. So, by
2
-out-of-
3
, in order to prove that the top adjunction is a Quillen equivalence it suffices to show that the two bottom vertical adjunctions are Quillen equivalences and as they are defined similarly, it suffices to prove that
((s_{\mathscr{C}})_{!},(s_{\mathscr{C}})^{*})
is a Quillen equivalence.

In order to simplify notation, we will henceforth denote the functor
s_{\mathscr{C}}:\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}(\mathscr{C})\to R\mathscr{C}
by
I:\mathscr{C}\to\hat{\mathscr{C}}
and notice the functor
I
is the identity on objects and for any two objects,
c,c^{\prime}
the map of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})\to\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\hat{\mathscr{C}}}(c,c^{\prime})
is the fibrant replacement in the
S
-localized injective model structure on
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.

Let
\widetilde{N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{c/}}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}
be the injectively fibrant replacement of the over-category
N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{c/}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}
and notice it is the fibrant replacement of
\{c\}:D[0]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}
in the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure (
2.3
). Moreover, the fiber of the derived unit map

\widetilde{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{c/}}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}I^{*}(\widetilde{N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{c/}})=N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}}\widetilde{N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{c/}}

over an object
c
is injectively equivalent to
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})\to\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\hat{\mathscr{C}}}(c,c^{\prime})
, which, by
4.73
, is the fibrant replacement in the
S
-localized injective model structure. Hence, the derived unit map is an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence over

N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
(
4.63
). This, in particular, implies that we have the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-contravariant equivalence

(4.81)

N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{/c}\times_{\hat{\mathscr{C}}}\mathscr{C}\simeq N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/c}.

We will use this observation to prove the left adjoint reflects equivalences. Let
Y\to Z
be an arbitrary map over
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
such that
Y\to Z
is an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence over
N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}
. By
4.52
, this is equivalent to
Y\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{/c}\to Z\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{/c}
being a diagonal
S
-localized injective equivalence. Now we also have injective equivalences

Y\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{/c}\cong Y\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}_{/c}\simeq Y\times_{N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/c}

where the first follows from the pasting property of pullbacks and the second follows from
4.81
. This, again by
4.52
, implies that
Y\to Z
is an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence over
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
.

Hence, in order to prove a Quillen equivalence, we only need to show the counit map
N_{\mathcal{D}}I_{!}N_{\mathcal{D}}I^{*}L\to L
is an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence over
N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}}
for all
S
-local
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations
L\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{C}
. We will prove that
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{k}(N_{\mathcal{D}}I_{!}N_{\mathcal{D}}I^{*}L\to L)
is an
S
-localized injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces and the fact that
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{k}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}})
is
S
-local (by
4.73
) along with
4.22
implies the desired result.

As
L\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{C}
is a
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration, we have an injective equivalence

(4.82)

\begin{tabular}[]{ccl}$\displaystyle\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{k}(L)$&$\displaystyle\simeq$&$\displaystyle\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{0}(L)\times_{\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{0}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}})}\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{k}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\hat{\mathscr{C}})$\\
&$\cong$&$\displaystyle\coprod_{c_{0},...,c_{k}}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c_{0}}L\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\hat{\mathscr{C}}}(c_{0},c_{1})\times...\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\hat{\mathscr{C}}}(c_{k-1},c_{k})$\end{tabular}.

Similarly we have

(4.83)

\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{k}(N_{\mathcal{D}}I_{!}N_{\mathcal{D}}I^{*}L)=\coprod_{c_{0},...,c_{k}}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c_{0}}L\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},c_{1})\times...\times\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c_{k-1},c_{k}).

The fact that the map
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c_{0},c_{1})\to\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\hat{\mathscr{C}}}(c_{0},c_{1})
is the fibrant replacement (by
4.73
) and that
\mathscr{C}
has Cartesian mapping objects (
4.68
) immediately implies the map from
4.83
to
4.82
is an
S
-localized equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.
∎

We now have the following result mirroring
4.80
.

Corollary 4.84
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
have a terminal object
t
. Let
S
be geometrically contractible set of cofibration of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. Let
G:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
be an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched functor of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories, such that the categories have Cartesian mapping
\mathcal{D}
-spaces (
4.68
) with
S
-stable objects (
4.74
). Then the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen adjunction, which is a Quillen equivalence whenever
G
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence (
4.66
).
Here both sides have the
S
-localized projective model structure.

Proof.

The fact that it is a Quillen adjunction follows directly from the fact that the restriction functor
G^{*}
preserves weak equivalences and fibrations. Now, if
G
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence, then we have the following diagram of Quillen adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{D},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))^{inj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}})^{\mathcal{D}-cov_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{D}})^{\mathcal{D}-cov_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}G_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}G^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{D}}}

\scriptstyle\bot

.

The vertical adjunctions are Quillen equivalences by
4.51
and the bottom is a Quillen equivalence by
4.80
and so, by
2
-out-of-
3
the top is a Quillen equivalence. The result now follows from again applying
2
-out-of-
3
for Quillen equivalences to the top Quillen equivalence combined with
4.49
and
4.48
.
∎

Remark 4.85
.

An alternative proof of
4.84
with somewhat different assumptions is given
[
Lur09a
, Proposition A.3.3.8]
. It is interesting to contrast the assumptions and notice the centrality of Cartesian mapping spaces and
S
-stable objects. Indeed, the condition that objects are
S
-stable is very similar to the
invertibility hypothesis
, also known as condition
(*)
, as given in
[
Lur09a
, Definition A.3.2.12]
.

The proof of
[
Lur09a
, Proposition A.3.3.8]
also uses an assumption very analogous so the existence of Cartesian mapping spaces as illustrated in the diagram in
[
Lur09a
, Bottom of Page 872]
, which is part of the proof of
[
Lur09a
, Proposition A.3.3.8]
. On a more concrete side, we will give a counter-example to
4.80
(and hence
4.84
) illustrating the necessity of Cartesian mapping spaces (or a similar condition) in
5.63
.

In
4.80
we proved that under the appropriate assumptions the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure is invariant under
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalences. How does the model structure behave with respect to
S
-localized level-wise complete Segal space equivalences? First we have the following lemma relating these two notions.

Lemma 4.86
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object
t
. Let
X,Y
be
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with weakly constant objects, Cartesian mapping spaces and
S
-stable objects. Let
f:X\to Y
be an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence, then
f
is an
S
-localized level-wise complete Segal space equivalence.

Proof.

By
3.48

X\to Y
is level-wise complete Segal space equivalent to a map of nerve
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{Y}
and
4.75

N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{Y}
is level-wise complete Segal space equivalent to
N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}_{X}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}_{Y}
and is
S
-local equivalent to the map
N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{X}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{Y}
. As all of these maps are
S
-localized level-wise complete Segal space equivalences (
4.42
),
X\to Y
is an
S
-localized level-wise complete Segal space equivalence if and only if
N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{X}\to N_{\mathcal{D}}R\mathscr{C}_{Y}
is one.

Now, by
4.75
, if
X\to Y
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence, then
R\mathscr{C}_{X}\to R\mathscr{C}_{Y}
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence, or, equivalently, a level-wise complete Segal space equivalence (
1.80
), which implies it is an
S
-localized level-wise complete Segal space equivalence (
4.42
).
∎

We would expect that under appropriate conditions on the objects
X,Y

S
-localized level-wise complete Segal space equivalences are in fact equivalent to
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalences, meaning
4.80
would then imply the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure is invariant under
S
-localized level-wise complete Segal space equivalences, which would provide a more precise analogue to
4.19
.

This leaves us with the question whether the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure could be invariant under more general
S
-localized level-wise complete Segal space equivalences that go beyond
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalences. The next result suggests that this should be very unlikely.

Proposition 4.87
.

Let
S
be a set of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces, such that for any
S
-localized level-wise CSS equivalence
g:X\to Y
the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{/X})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{/Y})^{\mathcal{D}-Cov_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen equivalence, where both sides have the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure. Then the
S
-localized injective model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
is right proper.

Proof.

Fix an
S
-localized injective equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces
g:X\to Y
and notice by direct computation
\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(X))=X
and similarly for
Y
. Then we have the following diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})_{/Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(g)_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(g)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{*}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{*}}

\scriptstyle\bot

The vertical adjunctions are Quillen equivalences by
4.15
and the top adjunction is a Quillen equivalence by assumption, which proves that the bottom is a Quillen equivalence for every map
g:X\to Y
. However, this is equivalent to the
S
-localized injective model structure being right proper
[
Rez02
, Proposition 2.5]
.
∎

Remark 4.88
.

The right properness assumption is very strong and is not satisfied by most examples of interest. Hence we needed a result such as
4.80
that still holds for relevant examples (
5.10
).

We can use
4.80
to improve
4.34
.

Theorem 4.89
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object
t
. Let
S
be a Cartesian geometrically contractible set of cofibration of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant and
S
-stable objects.
Let
p:R\to X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-right or
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration. Then the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{(lev_{CSS})_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/R})^{(lev_{CSS})_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen adjunction. Here both sides have the
S
-localized level-wise CSS model structure (
4.16
) on the over-category.

Proof.

Evidently
p^{*}
preserves cofibrations and so we only need to prove it preserves trivial cofibrations. For that we need an additional construction. Let
g:X\to\hat{X}
be a fibrant replacement of
X
in the
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure. Then, by
4.80
, there exists an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-right fibration
\hat{R}\to\hat{X}
such that there is an injective equivalence
R\simeq\hat{R}\times_{\hat{X}}X
.

Now, let
A\to B
be a trivial cofibration over
X
. We want to prove that
p^{*}(A\to B)
is a trivial cofibration over
R
. By direct computation

p^{*}(A\to B)=p^{*}A\to p^{*}B=R\times_{X}A\to R\times_{X}B\simeq\hat{R}\times_{\hat{X}}A\to\hat{R}\times_{\hat{X}}B.

The result now follows from the fact that this map is a trivial cofibration over
\hat{X}
, by
4.34
, and the fact that
g_{!}
is the left Quillen functor of the Quillen equivalence in
4.80
and hence reflects weak equivalences.
∎

Finally, we can use similar ideas to get an adjunction of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal spaces.

Corollary 4.90
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object
t
. Let
S
be a Cartesian geometrically contractible set of cofibration of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant and
S
-stable objects.
Let
p:R\to X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-right or
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration. Then the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{\mathcal{D}-CSS_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/R})^{\mathcal{D}-CSS_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen adjunction. Here both sides have the
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched CSS model structure (
4.42
) on the over-category.

Proof.

By
1.83
and
4.42
, the
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure can be obtained by localizing the
S
-localized level-wise Segal space model structure with respect to the maps
D[0]\to\mathscr{D}\mathrm{isc}(E[1])\to X
and
F[t,0]\to F[d,0]\to X
. The first map is already covered by
4.89
and so, by
4.9
, it suffices to prove
p^{*}F[t,0]\to p^{*}F[d,0]
is an
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalence.

As
X
has weakly constant objects we can assume that
F[d,0]\to X
factors through
F[d,0]\to D[0]\xrightarrow{\ \{x\}\ }X
. Hence, we have
p^{*}(F[t,0]\to F[d,0])=\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}R\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}R\times F[d,0]
, which by definition is an equivalence in the
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure (
1.83
) and so also in the
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure (
4.42
).
∎

5.
(Segal) Cartesian Fibrations of
(\infty,n)
-Categories

In this section we apply the previous work to the particular case of interest:
(Segal) Cartesian fibrations of
(\infty,n)
-Categories
, for models of
(\infty,n)
-categories based on simplicial presheaves. We first review the relevant theories of
(\infty,n)
-categories (
Section

5.1
) before moving on to the main results regarding
(\infty,n)
-categorical fibrations (
Section

5.2
), ending with some low-dimensional examples (
Section

5.3
).

5.1.
Theories of
(\infty,n)
-Categories

Let us review the relevant models of
(\infty,n)
-categories as certain simplicial presheaves.
For that we first recall the
\Theta
construction. For a category
\mathscr{C}
let
\Theta\mathscr{C}
be the category with objects of the form
[k](c_{1},...,c_{k})
, where
k\geq 0
and
c_{1},...,c_{k}
are objects in
\mathscr{C}
. Moreover, the morphisms are given as

\mathrm{Hom}_{\Theta\mathscr{C}}([k](c_{1},...,c_{k}),[l](d_{1},...,d_{l}))=\coprod_{\delta:[k]\to[l]}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\prod_{j=\delta(i-1)+1}^{\delta(i)}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(c_{i},d_{j}).

The
\Theta
construction is originally due to Joyal
[
Joy97
]
. For a nice review of the
\Theta
-construction see
[
Rez10a
, Subsection 3.2]
.

Definition 5.1
.

Define
\Theta_{0}
as the terminal category and
\Theta_{k+1}=\Theta\Theta_{k}
. Notice
\Theta_{1}={\mathbbe{\Delta}}
.

We want to study models of
(\infty,n)
-categories via
\Theta_{n}
-spaces with a model structure on the category
s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{n})
. Let
(-)[1]:s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{n})\to s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{n-1})
be the functor that takes a
\Theta_{n}
-space
X
to the
\Theta_{n-1}
-space
X[1]
with value
(X[1])(\theta)=X[1](\theta)
.
Moreover, for a
\Theta_{n}
-space
X
, let
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}(X)
, the
underlying simplicial space
, be the simplicial space defined as

(5.2)

\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}(X)[n]=X[n]([0],...,[0]).

For more details see
[
Rez10a
, Subsection 4.1]
, where it is denoted
T^{*}
. Finally, there is a functor
\Sigma:s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{n-1})\to s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{n})
, given by
\Sigma(A)=V[1](A)
, where
V
is the
intertwining functor

[
Rez10a
, Subsection 4.4]
.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
X
be a
\Theta_{n}
-space. Let
(\infty,0)
-
\Theta
-spaces be Kan complexes and define an
(\infty,n)
-
\Theta
-space
X
inductively as:

(1)

Fibrancy:

X
is injectively fibrant.

(2)

Segal:

X
satisfies the Segal condition, meaning for every object
[n](c_{1},...,c_{n})
in
\Theta_{n}
, the map

X[n](c_{1},...,c_{n})\to X[1](c_{1})\times_{X[0]}...\times_{X[0]}X[1](c_{n})

is an equivalence of spaces.

(3)

Completeness:
The underlying simplicial space
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}X
(
5.2
), which is a Segal space by the previous condition, is complete.

(4)

Enrichment:
The
\Theta_{n-1}
-space
X[1]
is an
(\infty,n-1)
-
\Theta
-space.

We now have the following theorem, with slightly different notation, due to Rezk
[
Rez10a
]
.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
n\geq 0
. There exists a left proper combinatorial Cartesian model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{n})
with the following specifications:

(1)

Cofibrations are monomorphisms.

(2)

Fibrant objects are
(\infty,n)
-
\Theta
-spaces.

Moreover, the model structure is obtained by localizing the injective model structure on
\Theta_{n}
-spaces with respect to the following inductively defined set of cofibrations:

•

If
n=0
, then the localizing set is empty.

•

The Segal morphisms
F[1](c_{1})\coprod_{F[0]}...F[1](c_{n})\to F[n](c_{1},...,c_{n})

•

The completeness map
F[0]\to\mathscr{D}\mathrm{isc}(E[1])

•

Maps of the form
\Sigma(f):\Sigma(A)\to\Sigma(B)
, where
f:A\to B
is a map of
\Theta_{n-1}
-spaces that defined
(\infty,n-1)
-
\Theta
-spaces.

We now want to use this to define a model of
(\infty,n)
-categories on diagrams of the form
\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k}
. In order to do that we need the following key result by Bergner and Rezk
[
BR20
, Corollary 7.2]
with somewhat different terminology.

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a small category with terminal object and
S
a set of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces such that the
S
-localized injective model structure on
\mathcal{D}
-spaces (
4.11
) is a model for
(\infty,n-1)
-categories. Then the
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure (
4.42
) is a model for
(\infty,n)
-categories. The model structure is obtained by localizing the injective model structure on
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with respect to the following morphisms

•

\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(S)\times D[n]
, where
n\geq 0
.

•

F[d,0]\times\mathscr{D}\mathrm{isc}(G[n])\to F[d,0]\times D[n]
, where
n\geq 2
and
d\in\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathcal{D}}
.

•

D[0]\to\mathscr{D}\mathrm{isc}(E[1])

•

D[0]\to F[d]
, where
d\in\mathrm{Obj}_{\mathcal{D}}

Notice, by
[
BR20
, Corollary 7.3]
, we can relate these model structures via simplicial Quillen equivalences.

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
n\geq 0
. There is a chain of simplicial Quillen equivalences of simplicial model categories
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}({\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}({\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-1}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}({\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-2}\times\Theta_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{n}}

,

where the categories have the model structures given by
5.5
or
5.4
. Moreover, only the right hand model structure is Cartesian.

In order to use these results we need to establish some notation with regard to localizing morphisms.

Notation 5.7
.

Fix the following notation for sets of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.

(1)

Let
S_{disc}
be the set of cofibrations in
s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k})
necessary to get the discreteness condition (if
n=k
then
S_{disc}
is empty).

(2)

Let
S_{Seg}
be the set of cofibrations in
s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k})
necessary to get the Segal condition. Moreover, let
S_{Seg,disc}=S_{Seg}\cup S_{disc}
.

(3)

Let
S_{CSS}
be the set of cofibrations in
s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k})
necessary to get the Segal and completeness condition. Moreover, let
S_{CSS,disc}=S_{CSS}\cup S_{disc}
.

We use the following terminology for the injectively fibrant
S
-local objects and the underlying quasi-category of
S
-local objects depending on the choice of
S
.

(5.8)

Localizing Set

Local Object

Notation

Quasi-Cat

S_{Seg}

n
-Segal Space

n
-Seg

\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}^{n}

S_{Seg,disc}

Segal
(\infty,n)
-Category

Seg\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}

\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}

S_{CSS}

n
-complete Segal space

n
-CSS

\textsc{css}^{n}

S_{CSS,disc}

(\infty,n)
-Category

\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}

\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}

In particular, a
theory of
(\infty,n)
-categories
is any choice of model structure in
5.6
and an
(\infty,n)
-category is a fibrant object in one of these model structures. Let us review the relevant properties of the localizing sets.

Remark 5.9
.

For a given subset
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
, the
S
-localized model structure is Cartesian if and only if
S\subseteq S_{CSS}

[
BR20
, Proposition 5.9]
. This implies that the
n
-Segal space and the
n
-complete Segal space model structures are always Cartesian and the (Segal)
(\infty,n)
-category model structure is Cartesian if and only if
k=n
. Thus, by
[
Hau15
, Theorem 1.1]
, the underlying quasi-categories are enriched over themselves in the sense of
[
GH15
]
. In particular, if
k=n
, then
\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}
is enriched over itself meaning it is an
(\infty,n+1)
-category in the sense of
[
GH15
, Definition 6.1.5]
.

Remark 5.10
.

Notice, these localizations are mostly not right proper. Indeed, even the Segal space model structure on simplicial spaces is not right proper
[
Ras17b
, Example 5.19]
. This fact, combined with
4.87
confirms what we mentioned in
4.88
, namely that we need some assumptions to get an invariance result such as
4.80
, which we apply to the context of
(\infty,n)
-categories in
5.38
.

Having done the review, we can move on to prove several results with regard to these localizations that are relevant in the next subsection. First we tackle geometric contractibility.

Lemma 5.11
.

The set of generating trivial cofibrations given in
5.4
or
5.5
are geometrically contractible.

Proof.

We will make several inductive arguments. First of all we prove the generating cofibration in
5.4
are geometrically contractible. Notice if
f
is a geometrically contractible map of
\Theta_{n-1}
-spaces, then
\Sigma f
is also contractible. Indeed,
|\Sigma f|\simeq\Sigma|f|
and the suspension of a contractible space is contractible.

Now, for a given object
[n](c_{1},...,c_{n})
in
\Theta_{n}
,
|F[n](c_{1},...,c_{n})|
,
|F[1](c_{1})|
and
|F[0]|
are contractible spaces and so

|F[1](c_{1})\coprod_{F[0]}...F[1](c_{n})|\to|F[n](c_{1},...,c_{n})|

is an equivalence of contractible spaces. Indeed, by definition of
|-|
(
4.2
) the image of every representable
\mathcal{D}
-space is contractible. Moreover,
|\mathscr{D}\mathrm{isc}(E[1])|
is contractible, hence
|D[0]|\to|E[1]|
is also a map of contractible spaces.

We will now move on to the trivial cofibrations given in
5.5
, assuming
S
is geometrically contractible. Notice, the product of geometrically contractible
\mathcal{D}
-spaces are geometrically contractible, which means it suffices to check the individual objects
D[n]
,
\mathscr{D}\mathrm{isc}(G[n])
,
\mathscr{D}\mathrm{isc}(E[1])
and
F[d,0]
are geometrically contractible, which follows by direct observation.
∎

Next, we move on to
S
-stability. For that we need the following lemma.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
X,Y,Z
be Segal spaces and
a:X\times Y\to Z
a map of Segal spaces, where
Z
has a distinguished object
z
. Then for a given object
x
in
X
, there exists an object
y
with
a(x,y)
equivalent to
z
in
Z
if and only if there exists an object
y
in the homotopy category
\mathrm{Ho}(Y)
, such that
a(x,y)
is isomorphic to
z
.

Proof.

Follows directly from the fact that a map is an equivalence in a Segal space if and only if it is an isomorphism in the homotopy category
[
Rez01
, Subsection 5.5]
.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
S\subseteq S_{CSS}
. Then every
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category
\mathscr{C}
has
S
-stable objects (
4.74
).

Proof.

Following
4.74
we need to prove that for a given
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category
\mathscr{C}
, the functor
\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}\to R\mathscr{C}
constructed in
4.73
gives us a bijection on equivalence classes of objects. Fix two objects
c,c^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}
. By
4.73
, the map
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})\to\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{R\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
is the fibrant replacement in the
S
-localized injective model structure, which, up to injective equivalence is obtained by applying the small object argument to the
\mathcal{D}
-space
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
with respect to the following classes of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces: injective equivalences, Segal morphisms and completeness morphism.

Hence, it suffices to show that all three classes of maps individually are
S
-stable. Injective equivalences are evident. Segal morphisms do not change the space
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
at all, as the map
G[n]_{0}\to F[n]_{0}
is the identity for
n\geq 2
. This leaves us with completeness maps. Hence, assume
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
is already local with respect to the Segal maps and denote the completion by
\widehat{\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})}
.

We want to prove that a morphism is a weak equivalence in the mapping
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
if and only if it is an equivalence in the mapping
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space
\widehat{\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})}
. By definition of completions, the space
\widehat{\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})}[t]
are the space of weak equivalences weak equivalences in the Segal space
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
, hence it suffices to prove an object is an equivalence in the Segal space
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
if and only if it is an equivalence in the completed complete Segal space
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\widehat{\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{et}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})}
.

Let
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}
be the Segal space enriched category with the same objects as
\mathscr{C}
and
\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})=\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
and notice the equivalences in
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}
coincide. Now, let
\mathrm{Ho}\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}
be the
2
-category with the same objects as
\mathscr{C}
and
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{Ho}\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})=\mathrm{Ho}\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})
, where the right hand
\mathrm{Ho}
is the homotopy category of the Segal space
\mathscr{U}\mathrm{nd}\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(c,c^{\prime})

[
Rez01
, Subsection 5.5]
. Then, by
5.12
, an object in the Segal space is an equivalence if and only if it is one in the homotopy category. However, the completion functor from a Segal space to the complete Segal space is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence
[
Rez01
, Theorem 7.7]
and so induces equivalent homotopy categories. Hence, we are done.
∎

Finally, we need the following result with regard to Cartesian localizations.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k}
and let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-space that is local with respect to
S_{Seg,disc}
. Let
A
be a
\mathcal{D}
-space and
X\to\hat{X}
be the fibrant replacement in the model structure for
(\infty,n)
-categories. Then
A\times X\to A\times\hat{X}
is an equivalence in the model structure for
(\infty,n)
-categories.

Proof.

By assumption
X
is already local with respect to
S_{disc}
and so the fibrant replacement
\hat{X}
with respect to
S_{CSS}
is already local with respect to
S_{CSS,disc}
. However, the
n
-complete Segal space model structure is Cartesian (
5.9
) and so the map
A\times X\to A\times\hat{X}
is an equivalence in the
n
-complete Segal space model structure and so, in particular, a equivalence in the model structure for
(\infty,n)
-categories.
∎

5.2.
Fibrations of
(\infty,n)
-Categories

In this section we will finally study fibrations of
(\infty,n)
-categories. Fix natural numbers
0\leq k\leq n<+\infty
and let
\mathcal{D}
be the category
\mathcal{D}=\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k}
. Notice this category has a terminal object
([0],[0],...,[0])
, where there are
n-k+1
copies of
[0]
, and we will hence implicitly use the fact that
\mathcal{D}
has a terminal object whenever necessary.

Throughout this section
S
is a set of cofibrations of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces, such that
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
(
5.7
). Finally, by
5.11
, all cofibrations in
S_{CSS,disc}
are geometrically contractible. We will now consider fibrations in the context of
(\infty,n)
-categories. Recall an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration, as defined in (
4.10
), comes with a model structure (
4.17
), which in our context takes the following form.

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space. There is a left proper combinatorial simplicial model structure on
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces over
X
, the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure, that satisfies the following conditions:

•

The cofibrations are monomorphisms.

•

The fibrant objects are
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations.

•

A map
A\to B
is an equivalence if and only if for all fibrant objects
L\to X
the induced map

\mathrm{Map}_{/X}(B,L)\to\mathrm{Map}_{/X}(A,L)

is a Kan equivalence of spaces.

•

A weak equivalence (fibration) between fibrant objects is a level-wise equivalence (injective fibration).

•

It is enriched over
S
-localized injective model structure if
S\subseteq S_{CSS}
and in particular if
n=k
.

Proof.

The existence and properties of the
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure follows from
4.17
. Notice the enrichment follows from the fact that
S
is Cartesian if and only if
S\subseteq S_{CSS}
, by
5.9
.
∎

For historical reasons several notions of
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations deserve their own name.

Notation 5.16
.

We will use the following terminology with regard to the model structure and its fibrant objects, depending on the choice of localizations.

Notice, using
5.15
, we have the following enrichments: The
(\infty,n)
-covariant model structure and the
(\infty,n)
-(complete) Segal covariant model structures are always enriched and the
(\infty,n)
-(Segal) coCartesian model structures are enriched if
k=n
.

Notice, the
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian model structure is in fact independent of our choice of
(\infty,n)
-categories.

Theorem 5.17
.

The chain of simplicial Quillen equivalences in
5.6
induces a chain of Quillen equivalences of
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian model structures
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}({\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n})_{/X_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}({\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-1}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}({\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-2}\times\Theta_{2})_{/X_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{P}(\Theta_{n})_{/X_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{n}}

,

where for each
0\leq i\leq n-1
we have
LX_{i}\simeq X_{i+1}
(or equivalently
X_{i}\simeq R_{i}X_{i+1}
). Notice only the right hand model structure is enriched over the model structure for
(\infty,n)
-categories.

Proof.

Direct application of
4.65
using
5.11
.
∎

Remark 5.18
.

There is a contravariant approach to
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations via
\mathcal{D}
-right fibrations. Accordingly, there is also a contravariant version to all the results we state in this section. Instead of repeating of them, we will give the following translation table and only give more details when necessary.
