We proceed towards studying the properties of the fibrant objects and weak equivalences in the model structures defined in
5.15
. First of all we can understand the fibrations by analyzing fibers, which follows from
4.28
.

Proposition 5.19
.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects and let
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
. Then an
(\infty,n)
-left fibrations
L\to X
is an
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-left fibration if and only it is fiber-wise
S
-local.

This in particular has the following implications.

Corollary 5.20
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
(\infty,n+1)
-category and
\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{C}
be an
(\infty,n)
-left fibration. Then
\mathscr{L}
is a (Segal)
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration if and only if for every object
c
in
\mathscr{C}
,
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}\mathscr{L}
is a (Segal)
(\infty,n)
-category.

Next, the fibrations over
(\infty,n+1)
-categories are not necessarily
(\infty,n+1)
-categories, but are quite close, as follows directly from
4.22
.

Proposition 5.21
.

Let
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
.
Let
X
be
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space such that
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}_{1}(X)
is
S
-local and let
p:L\to X
be an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant fibration. Then
L
is a complete Segal object in
S
-local
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.

Remark 5.22
.

5.21
implies that the domain
L
satisfies all conditions of an
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space except for the condition that
\mathscr{V}\mathrm{al}(L)
is homotopically constant.

This has the following direct application.

Corollary 5.23
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-category and
p:\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{C}
a Segal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration. Then
\mathscr{L}
is an
(n+1)
-Segal space.

Corollary 5.24
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
(\infty,n+1)
-category and
p:\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{C}
an
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration. Then
\mathscr{L}
is an
(n+1)
-complete Segal space.

We move on to the Yoneda lemma.

Theorem 5.25
.

Let
W
be an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal space. Then
W_{x/}=D[0]\times_{W}W^{D[1]}
(
2.28
) is the
(\infty,n)
-covariant fibrant replacement of the object
x
. Moreover, if
W
is a (Segal)
(\infty,n+1)
-category, then
W_{x/}\to X
is in fact (Segal)
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian.

Proof.

Direct result of
2.30
,
2.31
and
4.43
.
∎

This in particular, gives us the more usual form of the Yoneda lemma for
(\infty,n+1)
-categories.

Corollary 5.26
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-category and
\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{C}
a Segal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration and
c
an object in
\mathscr{C}
. Then we have an equivalence of Segal
(\infty,n)
-categories

\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}\mathscr{L}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{C}_{c/},\mathscr{L})

Given the notational differences, it is instructive to state the contravariant case separately, with the proof being analogous

Theorem 5.27
.

Let
W
be an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal space. Then
W_{/x}=W^{D[1]}\times_{W}D[0]
(
2.33
) is the
(\infty,n)
-contravariant fibrant replacement of
x
. In particular, if
\mathscr{C}
is a (Segal)
(\infty,n+1)
-category and
c
an object, then
\mathscr{C}_{c/}
is in fact (Segal)
(\infty,n)
-Cartesian. Hence, for every Segal
(\infty,n)
-Cartesian fibration
\mathscr{R}\to\mathscr{C}
we have an equivalence of Segal
(\infty,n)
-categories

\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{c}\mathscr{R}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{/\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{C}_{/c},\mathscr{R})

We move on to weak equivalences between
(\infty,n)
-left fibrations.

Theorem 5.28
.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects and let
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
.
Let
f:L\to L^{\prime}
be a map of
(\infty,n)
-left fibration over
X
. Then
f
is an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-covariant equivalence if and only if for every object
x
in
X
, the map on fibers
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}L\to\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{x}L^{\prime}
is an
S
-localized equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces. In particular,
f
is

•

a Segal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian equivalence if and only if it is a fiber-wise Segal
(\infty,n)
-category equivalence.

•

an
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian equivalence if and only if it is a fiber-wise
(\infty,n)
-category equivalence.

Proof.

Direct application of
4.63
combined with the characterization of localizations in
5.16
.
∎

This in particular has the following corollary, which can be seen as a generalization of the result
[
Rez01
, Theorem 7.7]
or
[
BR20
, Proposition 8.17]
from Segal spaces to Segal fibrations.

Remark 5.29
.

Recall that a map of Segal
(\infty,n)
-categories is a
Dwyer-Kan equivalence
if it is essentially surjective and for any two objects the induced map on mapping spaces is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence of Segal
(\infty,n-1)
-categories. This corresponds to the
S_{CSS,disc}
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalences (
4.66
).

Corollary 5.30
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-category. Then a map of Segal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibrations
f:\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
is an
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian equivalence if and only if it is a fiber-wise Dwyer-Kan equivalence of Segal
(\infty,n)
-categories.

Proof.

By
5.28

f
is an
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian equivalence if and only if it is a fiber-wise an
(\infty,n)
-categorical equivalence, which by
[
BR20
, Proposition 8.17]
is equivalent to being a Dwyer-Kan equivalence of Segal
(\infty,n)
-categories.
∎

We can generalize this result to maps that are not fibrations.

Notation 5.31
.

In
1.45
we define
\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
as the left adjoint to the inclusion functor
\mathrm{Diag}_{*}:s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
. In the standard categorical literature this left adjoint is often known as
groupoidification
and denoted
(-)^{grpd}
or
(-)^{\simeq}
. We will hence use the notation
(-)^{grpd}
in this section to match our notation in this regard.

Now
4.64
immediately gives us the following result.

Theorem 5.32
.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects and let
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
.
Let
f:Y\to Z
be a map of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces over
X
. For every object
x
in
X
let
R_{x}\to X
be fibrant replacement in the
(\infty,n)
-contravariant model structure. Then
f
is an
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-covariant equivalence if and only if
(Y\times_{X}R_{x})^{grpd}\to(Z\times_{X}R_{x})^{grpd}
is an
S
-localized equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces for all objects
x
in
X
.

In particular,

•

f
is a Segal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian equivalence if and only if
(Y\times_{X}R_{x})^{grpd}\to(Z\times_{X}R_{x})^{grpd}
is a Segal
(\infty,n)
-category equivalence.

•

f
is an
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian equivalence if and only if
(Y\times_{X}R_{x})^{grpd}\to(Z\times_{X}R_{x})^{grpd}
is an
(\infty,n)
-category equivalence.

Combining this result with
5.27
gives us the following concrete application.

Corollary 5.33
.

Let
W
be be an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal space and let
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
. Then a morphism
f:Y\to Z
over
W
is an
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-covariant equivalence if and only if
(Y\times_{W}W_{/x})^{grpd}\to(Z\times_{W}W_{/x})^{grpd}
is an
S
-localized equivalence of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces for all objects
x
.

In particular, if
\mathscr{C}
is a Segal
(\infty,n)
-category and
f:\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{E}
over
\mathscr{C}
, then

•

f
is a Segal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian equivalence if and only if
(\mathscr{D}\times_{\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{C}_{/c})^{grpd}\to(\mathscr{E}\times_{\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{C}_{/c})^{grpd}
is a Segal
(\infty,n)
-category equivalence for all objects
c
in
\mathscr{C}
.

•

f
is an
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian equivalence if and only if
(\mathscr{D}\times_{\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{C}_{/c})^{grpd}\to(\mathscr{E}\times_{\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{C}_{/c})^{grpd}
is an
(\infty,n)
-category equivalence for all objects
c
in
\mathscr{C}
.

We move on to study the
(\infty,n)
-twisted arrow
construction.

Proposition 5.34
.

Let
W
be an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal space, then
\mathrm{Tw}(W)\to W^{op}\times W
(as defined in
2.35
) is an
(\infty,n)
-left fibration. Moreover, let
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
. If for all objects
x,y
in
W
the
\mathcal{D}
-space
\mathrm{map}_{W}(x,y)
is
S
-localized, then
\mathrm{Tw}(W)\to W^{op}\times W
is an
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration.

Proof.

The first sentence follows from
2.35
, the second from
4.44
.
∎

In the particular case of (Segal)
(\infty,n+1)
-category gives us the following result.

Corollary 5.35
.

If
\mathscr{C}
is a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-category, then
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{C}^{op}\times\mathscr{C}
is a Segal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration, which is an
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration if
\mathscr{C}
is an
(\infty,n+1)
-category.

Finally, we can use the
(\infty,n)
-twisted arrow construction to study the source and target fibrations.

Proposition 5.36
.

Let
W
be an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Segal space, then the target fibration
\mathrm{Tw}(W)\to W
(
2.36
) is an
(\infty,n+1)
-left fibration and the source fibration
\mathrm{Tw}(W)^{op}\to W
(
2.36
) is an
(\infty,n+1)
-right fibration. Moreover, if
S\subseteq S_{CSS,disc}
and for all objects
x,y
the
\mathcal{D}
-space
\mathrm{map}_{W}(x,y)
is
S
-localized, then the target fibration
\mathrm{Tw}(W)\to W
(source fibration
\mathrm{Tw}(W)^{op}\to W
) is an
S
-localized
(\infty,n+1)
-left fibrations (
S
-localized
(\infty,n+1)
-right fibration).

Proof.

The first sentence follows from
2.36
and
2.37
. The second from
4.45
.
∎

This, in particular, gives us the following concrete result.

Corollary 5.37
.

If
\mathscr{C}
is a Segal
(\infty,n)
-category, then the target fibration
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathscr{C}
is a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-coCartesian fibration which is an
(\infty,n+1)
-coCartesian fibration if
\mathscr{C}
is an
(\infty,n)
-category. Similarly, the source fibration
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathscr{C})^{op}\to\mathscr{C}
is a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-Cartesian fibration, which is a
(\infty,n+1)
-Cartesian fibration if
\mathscr{C}
is an
(\infty,n)
-category.

We now move on to invariance properties.

Theorem 5.38
.

Let
g:X\to Y
be a map of
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces. Then the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/Y})^{(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen adjunction, where both sides have
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-covariant model structures. Moreover, the adjunction is a Quillen equivalence under the following conditions:

•

If
g
is a level-wise complete Segal space equivalence.

•

If
g
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence and
X,Y
have weakly constant objects.

•

If
g
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence and
X
and
Y
have weakly constant objects and Cartesian mapping spaces (
4.68
).

•

If
g
is an
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalence and
X,Y
have weakly constant objects and Cartesian mapping spaces.

Proof.

The fact that
(g_{!},g^{*})
is an adjunction is proven in
4.19
. Now, if
g
is a level-wise complete Segal space equivalence, then
(g_{!},g^{*})
is a Quillen equivalence by
4.19
. If
g
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence between
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with weakly constant objects, then it is a level-wise complete Segal space equivalence by
1.80
and so the result follows.

Finally, if
g
is an
S
-localized Dwyer-Kan equivalence or
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space equivalence between
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with weakly constant objects and Cartesian mapping spaces, then the result follows from
4.80
. Notice, here we used the fact that Cartesian mapping spaces are already
S
-stable, by
5.13
. This covers the last two items and hence we are done.
∎

Remark 5.39
.

Most assumptions on the localizing set
S
that we used in
Section

4
are already satisfied by the localizing maps we are using in this section (
5.7
).
5.38
is in fact an exception where the Cartesian mapping space assumption is necessary and does not always hold. For a concrete counter example using
3
-fold complete Segal spaces see
5.63
.

In the case of Segal
(\infty,n)
-categories we can simplify the statement.

Corollary 5.40
.

Let
g:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
be Dwyer-Kan equivalence of Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-categories. Then the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{(Seg)coCart_{(\infty,n)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{D}})^{(Seg)coCart_{(\infty,n)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen equivalence if
g
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence of
(\infty,n+1)
-categories (
5.29
).
Here both sides have the (Segal)
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian model structure.

Proof.

By definition
\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D}
have weakly constant objects and by
5.14

\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{D}
have Cartesian mapping spaces. So,
g
satisfies the third condition of
5.38
.
∎

Having discussed invariance we move on to various exponentiability properties. First we can use
4.35
to get the following result with regard to exponentiability of
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-left fibrations.

Theorem 5.41
.

Let
S\subseteq S_{CSS}
and let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects. Then for any
(\infty,n)
-right fibration
R\to X
, the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Quillen adjunction. Here both sides have the
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-covariant model structure.

This gives us the following valuable case for
(\infty,n)
-categories.

Corollary 5.42
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-category. Then for any
(\infty,n)
-right fibration
p:\mathscr{R}\to\mathscr{C}
, the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{CScov_{(\infty,n)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{CScov_{(\infty,n)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen adjunction. Here both sides have the
(\infty,n)
-complete Segal covariant model structure.

It is worth explicitly pointing out the Cartesian case that should be the most useful one.

Corollary 5.43
.

Let
n=k
and let
\mathscr{C}
be a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-category. Then for any
(\infty,n)
-right fibration
p:\mathscr{R}\to\mathscr{C}
, the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{(Seg)coCart_{(\infty,n)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{(Seg)coCart_{(\infty,n)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen adjunction. Here both sides have the (Segal)
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian model structure.

We now move on to the exponentiability of fibrations of
(\infty,n)
-categories. Combining
4.89
and
4.90
gives us the following result.

Theorem 5.44
.

Let
S\subseteq S_{CSS}
and let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant and
S
-stable objects.
Let
p:R\to X
be an
(\infty,n)
-right or
(\infty,n)
-left fibration. Then the adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{(lev_{CSS})_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/R})^{(lev_{CSS})_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{\mathcal{D}-CSS_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/R})^{\mathcal{D}-CSS_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

are Quillen adjunctions. Here, in the first adjunction both sides have the
S
-localized level-wise CSS model structure (
4.16
) on the over-category and in the second adjunction both sides have the
S
-localized
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal space model structure (
4.42
).

Restricting to Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-categories and applying
5.13
to this theorem gives us the following important corollary.

Corollary 5.45
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-category. Let
p:\mathscr{R}\to\mathscr{C}
be an
(\infty,n)
-right or
(\infty,n)
-left fibration. Then the adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{(n+1)-Seg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{R}})^{(n+1)-Seg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{(n+1)-CSS}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{R}})^{(n+1)-CSS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

are Quillen adjunctions. Here the top has the
(n+1)
-Segal space model structure and the bottom the
(n+1)
-complete Segal space model structure on the over-category.

Again, it is valuable to make the most useful case,
n=k
, more explicit.

Corollary 5.46
.

Let
n=k
and
\mathscr{C}
be an
(\infty,n+1)
-category and
p:\mathscr{R}\to\mathscr{C}
an
(\infty,n)
-right or
(\infty,n)
-left fibration. Then the adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{R}})^{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{C}})^{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathscr{R}})^{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

are Quillen adjunctions. Here the top has the Segal
(\infty,n+1)
-category model structure and the bottom the
(\infty,n+1)
-category model structure.

Remark 5.47
.

This result can be seen as a generalization of the fact that in the
(\infty,1)
-categorical setting Cartesian fibrations are
exponentiable
, which has been observed in
[
Lur09a
]
, but also
[
Ras21a
]
, and is in fact an important aspect of the work in
[
AF20
]
.

We move on to the Grothendieck construction for
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-left fibrations. For that we need the following lemma.

Lemma 5.48
.

The functor
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}:\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))\to s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
takes projective fibrations to injective fibrations.

Proof.

By
[
BR13b
, Corollary 4.5]

\mathcal{D}=\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k}
is an elegant Reedy category and so, by
[
BR13b
, Proposition 3.15]
, the injective model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
coincides with the Reedy model structure on
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
. Hence, it suffices to prove that for every projective natural transformation
\alpha:G\to H
,
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha:s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G\to s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}H
is a Reedy fibration. By definition of the Reedy model structure we need to prove
s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}\alpha:s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}G\to s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}H
has the lifting condition with respect to the maps

\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A)\to\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(B)\square\partial D[n]\to D[n],

where
A\to B
is a trivial cofibration of
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.
By adjunction
(s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}},s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}})
this is equivalent to

(5.49)

s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(A)\to\mathscr{V}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{mb}(B)\square\partial D[n]\to D[n])

being a trivial cofibration in the projective model structure on
\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))
.

By
3.36

s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}
commutes with tensor over
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-spaces and so the map
5.49
is an equivalence if and only if

\{A\}\to\{B\}\square s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\partial D[n]\to D[n])

is an equivalence, which means it suffices to prove that
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\partial D[n]\to D[n])
is a projective cofibration. For
n\geq 2
,
s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\partial D[n]\to D[n])
is the identity. For the other two cases we have

•

s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\emptyset\to D[0])=\{\emptyset\}\to\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c_{0},-)
, where
\{c_{0}\}:D[0]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
.

•

s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}}(\partial D[1]\to D[1])=\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c_{0},-)\coprod\underline{\mathrm{Map}}(c_{1},-)\to D[1]\times_{\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C})}\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}(N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{/-})
, where
\{c_{0}\to c_{1}\}:D[1]\to N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}
.

Both of which are evidently projective cofibrations.
∎

Applying
5.48
to
4.53
gives us the Grothendieck construction for
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-left fibrations.

Theorem 5.50
.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects, which, in particular, includes (Segal)
(\infty,n+1)
-categories. Then we have the following diagram of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Quillen equivalences.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{inj_{S}})^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}})^{(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{inj_{S}})^{proj}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}X})^{(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{D}}X})^{(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})_{/X})^{(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(r_{X})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(r_{X})^{*}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u_{X})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u_{X})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})^{*}}

,

where the middle row has the
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-covariant model structure the other ones have the projective model structure on the
S
-localized injective model structure. Moreover, the Quillen equivalence is an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Quillen equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched model structures if
S\subseteq S_{CSS}
, which in particular always holds if
k=n
.

In order to avoid having to draw the diagram in
5.50
several times we state the special cases in a more succinct form.

Corollary 5.51
.

The diagram of Quillen equivalences in
5.50
hold in particular with the following substitutions.

S

inj_{S}

(cov_{(\infty,n)})_{S}

\emptyset

inj

cov_{(\infty,n)}

S_{Seg,disc}

Seg_{(\infty,n)}

SegcoCart_{(\infty,n)}

S_{CSS,disc}

CSS_{(\infty,n)}

coCart_{(\infty,n)}

,

where the equivalence of the two last rows is one of enriched model structures if and only if
k=n
.

We can again use this to get an equivalence of underlying quasi-categories. Motivated by
4.55
, we will denote the underlying quasi-category of the
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-covariant model structure over
X
by
\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/X}
. Now
4.57
gives us the following result.

Corollary 5.52
.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects, which in particular includes (Segal)
(\infty,n+1)
-categories. Then we have the following equivalence of quasi-categories
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{D}}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{D}}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\hskip-2.8903pt\int_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(r_{X})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{D}/\mathscr{C}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(r_{X})^{*}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u_{X})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(u_{X})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})_{!}}

\scriptstyle\bot

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{X})^{*}}

.

which is an equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]
-enriched quasi-categories and equivalent to the functor quasi-category
\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{Q}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]}}(\mathscr{C},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}])
if
S\subseteq S_{CSS}
, which holds in particular if
n=k
.

In particular, we get the following direct equivalences.

Corollary 5.53
.

Let
X
be a
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial space with weakly constant objects.
There is an equivalence of quasi-categories
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D}))[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

,

Moreover, if
S\subseteq S_{CSS}
(which holds in particular if
k=n
) then there is an equivalence
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]
-enriched quasi-categories
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{Q}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]}}(\mathscr{C}_{X},s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{\mathcal{D}/X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

.

Corollary 5.54
.

The diagram of Quillen equivalences in
5.52
and the direct equivalences
5.53
hold in particular with the following substitutions.

S

s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]

\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/X}

\emptyset

s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]

\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/X}

S_{Seg}

\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}^{n}

\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{(\infty,n)}

S_{CSS}

\textsc{css}^{n}

\mathscr{C}\mathscr{S}\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}_{(\infty,n)}

.

We will make two further equivalences that follow from
5.53
more explicit. Let
k=n
and
S=S_{Seg}
. Then,
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]
is the quasi-category of Segal
(\infty,n)
-categories
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}
(
5.8
) and
\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/X}
is the quasi-category of Segal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibrations,
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathrm{co}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}_{(\infty,n)/X}
, and
5.53
gives us the Segal
(\infty,n)
-category enriched equivalence
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}}(\mathscr{C}_{X},\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}\mathrm{eg}\mathrm{co}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}_{(\infty,n)/X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

.

The next case is even more interesting. Again, let
k=n
and
S=S_{CSS}
. Then
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})[\mathcal{W}^{-1}_{S}]
is the quasi-category of
(\infty,n)
-categories
\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}
(
5.8
) and
\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)/X}
is the quasi-category of
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibrations,
\mathrm{co}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}_{(\infty,n)/X}
. Moreover, by
[
GH15
, Definition 6.1.5]
,
\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}
-enriched categories are precisely
(\infty,n+1)
-categories.

Now, for a given
(\infty,n+1)
-category
X
, the corresponding
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched category
\mathscr{C}_{X}
is an
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched Dwyer-Kan equivalence of
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched complete Segal spaces (
3.48
), which correspond to fully faithful and essentially surjective morphisms of
(\infty,n+1)
-categories (in the sense of
[
GH15
, Definition 5.3.1, Definition 5.3.3]
) and so an equivalence of
(\infty,n+1)
-categories
[
GH15
, Corollary 5.5.4]
, giving us an equivalence of
(\infty,n+1)
-categories

(5.55)

\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)}}(\mathscr{C}_{X},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)})\simeq\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)}}(X,\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}).

Now, combining the equivalence in
5.55
with the result
5.53
with a focus on
(\infty,n+1)
-categories, we get the following result.

Corollary 5.56
.

Let
k=n
,
S=S_{CSS}
and
\mathscr{C}
be an
(\infty,n+1)
-category. Then the equivalence in
5.53
induces an equivalence of
(\infty,n+1)
-categories
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{co}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}_{(\infty,n)/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

and similarly an equivalence of
(\infty,n+1)
-categories
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}_{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)}}(\mathscr{C}^{op},\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}_{(\infty,n)/\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

.

This result is in some sense the most common form of the Grothendieck construction that we would expect in the setting of
(\infty,n)
-categories.

Example 5.57
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
(\infty,n+1)
-category. Then, following
3.57
, the twisted arrow
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration (
5.35
) corresponds (under the correspondence given in
5.56
) to the mapping object functor

\underline{\mathrm{Map}}_{\mathscr{C}}(-,-):\mathscr{C}^{op}\times\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}.

Similarly, using
3.58
, the target
(\infty,n+1)
-coCartesian fibration and the source
(\infty,n+1)
-Cartesian fibration (
5.37
) correspond, again via
5.56
, to
over-category functors
and
under-category functors

\mathscr{C}_{/-}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)},

\mathscr{C}_{-/}:\mathscr{C}^{op}\to\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)},

respectively.

We will end this section with an analysis of
universal
S
-localized
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration
and, in particular, the
universal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration
. Denote by
\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)}
the sub-right fibration of
\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{(all)}}_{/\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}}}
consisting of
S
-localized
(\infty,n)
-left fibrations. Notice, by
4.59
, the map of quasi-categories
(\textsc{css}_{s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}})_{/\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})^{S}}}\to\mathcal{L}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{ib}^{S}_{(\infty,n)}
is an equivalence. Applying this to the case
S=S_{CSS,disc}
and using notation in
5.8
we get the following special case.

Corollary 5.58
.

The map
(-)^{*}\pi_{*}:(\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n+1)})_{/\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}}\to\mathrm{co}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}_{(\infty,n)}
is an equivalence of
(\infty,n+1)
-categories.

Remark 5.59
.

Applying
4.60
to
5.58
it follows that there is a
universal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration
of the form
\pi_{*}:(\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)})_{D[0]/}\to\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}
. Moreover, for every
(\infty,n)
-left fibration
p:L\to\mathscr{C}
, there is a (homotopically unique) pullback
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)})_{D[0]/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}_{D[0]/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{D}}\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{N_{\mathcal{D}}s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}

.

that factors through
\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,n)}
if and only if
p
is
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian.

Again,
[
Ras21c
, Proposition 2.4]
implies the following result regarding univalence.

Corollary 5.60
.

The universal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration is univalent.

Remark 5.61
.

The study of universal coCartesian fibrations and their relation to univalence has a very rich history.

(1)

The underlying groupoid of the universal
(\infty,0)
-coCartesian fibration has been studied under names, such as
“classifying space of spaces"
. It is in fact one of the first examples of univalent morphisms in higher category theory, due to the work by Kapulkin and Lumsdaine
[
KL12
]
.

(2)

The actual universal
(\infty,0)
-coCartesian fibration is commonly known as the
universal left fibration

[
Cis19
,
Lur09a
,
Ras21c
]
, again the univalence thereof was well-established.

(3)

The universal
(\infty,1)
-coCartesian fibration was studied abstractly in
[
Lur09a
, Subsection 3.3.2]
. Moreover, it has been studied via internal
\infty
-categories (also known as
complete Segal objects
) in
[
Ste20
]
and
[
Ras17a
]
.

Notice this notion of univalence is not necessarily appropriate (as discussed in
[
Ras21c
, Section 7]
for the universal
(\infty,1)
-Cartesian fibration) and we would expect a more refined definition of univalence more suitable for the
(\infty,n)
-categorical setting. See
Section

0.5
for more details.

5.3.
Low-Dimensional Examples

In this last subsection we consider several low-dimensional cases of the results in
Section

5.2
more explicitly. We then use those examples to construct counter-examples, justifying the necessity of several conditions.

Let us start with the lowest possible case. If
n=0
, then
\mathcal{D}=\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k}
is necessarily the terminal category and
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})=\mathscr{S}
. In that case, the
\mathcal{D}
-covariant model structure is simply the covariant model structure for simplicial spaces, studied extensively in
[
Ras17b
]
and reviewed in
Section

1.7
. Notice, we can use this example in particular to observe that the Segal condition in
5.26
and
5.27
is necessary when deducing that
X^{D[1]}\times_{X}D[0]
is the
(\infty,1)
-covariant fibrant replacement. Indeed a counter-example for the case
X=G[2]
is given in
[
Ras17b
, Remark 3.48]
. As, according to
5.7
, the localizing set for
n=0
is empty, we move on to the next level to get interesting examples.

Let us move on to the case
n=1
. In that case
\mathcal{D}=\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k}={\mathbbe{\Delta}}
and so
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})=s\mathscr{S}
. Hence, the possible localizing sets (as described in
5.7
) are:

S_{Seg}=\{G[n]\to F[n]:n\geq 2\}

S_{CSS}=S_{Seg}\cup\{F[0]\to E[1]\}

S_{disc}=\emptyset

Let use the notation
ss\mathscr{S}=s\mathscr{P}({\mathbbe{\Delta}}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}})
for the category of bisimplicial spaces. Then for every bisimplicial space
X
, we have two
S
-localized
(\infty,1)
-covariant model structure on bisimplicial spaces over
X
: The (Segal)
(\infty,1)
-coCartesian model structure. By
5.15
both model structures are left proper, combinatorial, simplicial and enriched over the (complete) Segal space model structures on simplicial spaces.

Notice several results in
Section

5
rely on the assumption that
X
has weakly constant objects. We want to show that this assumption is in fact necessary which we illustrate in the following example.

Example 5.62
.

Let
F[1]
be the simplicial space embedded vertically in bisimplicial spaces. Then by
4.18
and
4.15
the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(ss\mathscr{S}_{/F[1]})^{coCart_{(\infty,1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\mathscr{S}_{/F[1]})^{CSS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is a Quillen equivalence. Let
G[2]\to F[1]
be the map given as
G[2]=F[1]\coprod_{F[0]}F[1]\to F[0]\coprod_{F[0]}F[1]\cong F[1]
. Then we have pullback squares (using
1.21
)
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces<0>}

,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces<1>}

and so the map is a fiber-wise complete Segal space, however, is not a complete Segal space fibration as
F[1]
is a complete Segal space, whereas
G[2]
is not (and in fact not even a Segal space). This implies that
G[2]\to F[1]
is a fiber-wise complete Segal space without being an
(\infty,1)
-coCartesian fibration justifying the weakly constant object assumption in
5.19
.

Next, notice the map
F[1]\coprod F[0]\to G[2]
is a fiber-wise equivalence (in fact identity) over
F[1]
, but evidently not an equivalence and so gives a justification for the weakly constant object assumption in
5.28
. Moreover, the two maps
<0>,<1>:F[0]\to F[1]
are already
(\infty,1)
-coCartesian fibrations and so equal to their fibrant replacement, this example also applies to
5.32
.

Next, the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(ss\mathscr{S}_{/G[2]})^{coCart_{(\infty,1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(ss\mathscr{S}_{/F[2]})^{coCart_{(\infty,1)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

induced by the equivalence
p:G[2]\to F[2]
is not a Quillen equivalence as the derived unit map of
<02>:F[1]\to F[2]
is
\partial F[1]\to F[1]
, which is not an equivalence justifying the weakly constant object requirement in
5.38
.

We can use a similar argument for another counter-example. Letting
p
be the
(\infty,1)
-right fibration
p=<02>:F[1]\to F[2]
the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(ss\mathscr{S}_{/F[2]})^{coCart_{(\infty,1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(ss\mathscr{S}_{/F[2]})^{coCart_{(\infty,1)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

is not a Quillen adjunction as it takes the equivalence
G[2]\to F[2]
over
F[2]
to the map
\partial F[1]\to F[1]
over
F[2]
, which is certainly not an equivalence giving us a counter-example to
5.41
without the weakly constant object assumption.

Having thoroughly covered the case
n=1
we move on to the case
n=2
. In this case we either have
\mathcal{D}=\Theta_{2}
or
\mathcal{D}={\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}
. If
\mathcal{D}=\Theta_{2}
, then the resulting model structure for
(\infty,2)
-categories is in fact Cartesian (
5.4
) and hence this model structure behaves similarly to the complete Segal space model structure and we will not discuss it further.

On the other hand, the
(\infty,2)
-categorical model structure on
s\mathscr{P}({\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2})
is given via
2
-fold complete Segal spaces, which is in fact not Cartesian and we will use it to show that the assumption of Cartesian mapping spaces in
4.80
, and its
(\infty,n)
-categorical application in
5.38
, is necessary.

Example 5.63
.

Fix a bisimplicial space
A
that is equivalent to the terminal object in the
2
-fold complete Segal space model structure and denote a choice of fibrant replacement by
A\to\hat{A}
. Let
\Sigma A
be the category enriched over bisimplicial spaces with two objects
0,1
and one non-trivial mapping object
\mathrm{Map}_{\Sigma A}(0,1)=A
. Define
\Sigma\hat{A}
similarly and notice the equivalence
A\to\hat{A}
gives us a localized equivalence of trisimplicial spaces
N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma A)\to N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma\hat{A})
.

Let
B
be a fibrant bisimplicial space in the
2
-fold complete Segal space model structure. Then the projection map
\hat{A}\times B\to\hat{A}
is an
(\infty,2)
-coCartesian fibration over
\hat{A}
. If
5.38
was valid, then the derived counit map, which is the top map of the pullback diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma A)\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma\hat{A})\times B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma\hat{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

would be an
(\infty,2)
-coCartesian equivalence over
N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}\Sigma\hat{A}
, which would in particular imply that the diagonal is an equivalence in the
2
-fold complete Segal space model structure (
4.18
).

Now we have
N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma A)=D[1]\times A\coprod_{\partial D[1]\times A}\partial D[1]\simeq D[1]\times A\coprod_{\partial D[1]\times A}\partial D[1]\times\hat{A}
. Hence,

(5.64)

\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma A\times B)=\Delta[1]\times A\times B\coprod_{\partial D[1]\times A}\partial D[1]\times\hat{A}

and similarly

(5.65)

\mathscr{D}\mathrm{iag}N_{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{2}}(\Sigma\hat{A}\times B)=\Delta[1]\times\hat{A}\times B\coprod_{\partial D[1]\times\hat{A}}\partial D[1]\times\hat{A}=\Delta[1]\times\hat{A}\times B

and so the map from
5.64
to
5.65
is simply the pushout product
(A\to\hat{A})\square(\emptyset\to B)\square(\partial\Delta[1]\to\Delta[1])
. This pushout product is generally not a trivial cofibration.

Indeed, for a concrete counter-example let
A=F[0,1]
and
B=F[1,0]
. By definition of the
2
-fold complete Segal space model structure (
5.5
), the map
F[0,0]\to F[0,1]
is an equivalence and
F[1,0]
is fibrant, but the map
F[1,0]\to F[1,1]
, which is the pushout product of
F[0,0]\to F[0,1]
with
\emptyset\to F[1,0]
is not an equivalence.

6.
What needs to be done?

In this last section we discuss work that is left to be done. We use the notation and the results from the previous sections.

(1)

Comparison with other Models of Fibrations:
In the
(\infty,2)
-categorical setting there are several alternative studies of fibrations using marked (and scaled) simplicial sets
[
Lur09b
,
GHL21b
,
AGS21
]
. We do know that our approach to fibrations does compare appropriately when restricted to
(\infty,1)
-categories (this is proven in
[
Ras17b
, Appendix B]
), however, it remains to be seen whether this generalizes to a comparison between
(\infty,2)
-categorical fibrations given in
Section

5.2
and scaled simplicial approach. This could be quite challenging as these fibrations use very different models of
(\infty,2)
-categories, which cannot be directly compared. However, there is the possibility of using recent results directly comparing
\Theta_{2}
-sets and
2
-complicial sets
[
BOR21
]
.

(2)

Fibrations for Lax Functors
As stated in
5.50
, the notion of Cartesian fibration given here corresponds to
pseudo-functors
rather than
lax functors
. This in contrast to work in
[
GHL21b
]
, which consider fibrations with different levels of laxity.

The notion
\mathcal{D}
-left fibration given here (
2.1
) is a direct generalization of left fibrations, which by its
(\infty,1)
-categorical nature is pseudo by definition. Hence, one valuable next step is to work out the appropriate generalization of level-wise left fibrations that is able to include the data of a lax functor.

One interesting possibility is to use the
(\infty,n)
-categorical twisted arrow construction (
5.35
/
5.57
) to study lax constructions, similar to what has been done in
[
GHN17
]
in the context of lax limits of
(\infty,1)
-categories.

(3)

Fibrations in
\Theta_{n}
-Spaces:

Starting with a simplicial presheaf model of
(\infty,n)
-categories, there are generally two methods to generalize it to a model of
(\infty,n+1)
-categories: Either we apply the
\Theta
-construction to the diagram category, and applying this line of thinking to complete Segal spaces precisely gives us
\Theta_{n}
-spaces
[
Rez10a
]
, or we take the product of the diagram with
{\mathbbe{\Delta}}
, and applying this to complete Segal spaces gives us
n
-fold complete Segal spaces
[
Bar05
]
.

From this perspective the results of this paper can be understood as applying the product with
{\mathbbe{\Delta}}
to a given diagram
\mathcal{D}
, leaving us with the question whether we can give an analogous construction on presheaf categories
s\mathscr{P}(\Theta\mathscr{D})
. It is expected that such a fibration for
\Theta\mathcal{D}
-spaces would require a very different construction, as for a given
\Theta\mathcal{D}
-spaces
X
, the analogue to the value for
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces (
1.41
) would just be a space
X[0]
and hence could not be used to define a fibration with fibers in
\mathcal{D}
-spaces.

(4)

Fibrations for Set-Valued Diagrams:

In
[
Ara14
]
Ara uses the theory of
Cisinski model categories

[
Cis06
]
to construct a model structure on
\Theta_{n}
-sets,
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n}^{op},\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et})
, and Quillen equivalences between
\Theta_{n}
-spaces and
\Theta_{n}
-sets, generalizing the Quillen equivalences between quasi-categories and complete Segal spaces due to Joyal and Tierney
[
JT07
]
.

We already know that we can adjust the Quillen equivalences by Joyal and Tierney to both prove an equivalence of
(\infty,0)
-coCartesian fibrations over quasi-categories and complete Segal spaces
[
Ras17b
, Appendix B]
and similarly an equivalence of
(\infty,1)
-coCartesian fibrations
[
Ras21b
]
.

The results of this work suggest two natural next steps: First, can we use a similar approach to Ara (possibly again via results in
[
Cis06
]
) to define fibrations for
\Theta_{n}
-set-enriched Segal spaces and prove it is equivalent to
\Theta_{n}
-space enriched Segal spaces studied here? Moreover, assuming we can develop a theory of fibrations of
\Theta_{n}
-spaces, as outlined in the previous item, can we construct an equivalent theory of fibrations of
\Theta_{n}
-sets, using similar methods?

(5)

Direct Grothendieck Construction:

In
Section

3.2
we construct a zigzag of Quillen equivalences between functor categories and fibrations over
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces with weakly constant objects. This zigzag already enables us to gain deep insight into various properties of localized
\mathcal{D}
-left fibrations and in particular
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibrations.

However, for many purposes it would be more advantageous to have a direct Quillen equivalence between functors and fibrations, similar to the
straightening construction

[
Lur09a
, Theorem 3.2.0.1]
. The key difference is that the straightening construction takes two steps as once. For a given map of simplicial sets
X\to S
it first constructs a simplicially enriched category
\mathfrak{C}[S]
, known as the
strictification

[
Lur09a
, Definition 1.1.5.1]
and then a simplicial enriched functor from
\mathfrak{C}[S]
valued in simplicial sets. Hence, this direct construction relies on the fact that there is a direct Quillen equivalence
\mathfrak{C}[-]
from quasi-categories to Kan enriched categories
[
Lur09a
, Theorem 2.2.5.1]
.

In order to generalize this construction we would need strong strictification results that take
\mathcal{D}
-simplicial spaces to
s\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{D})
-enriched categories, which does not exist yet and might not be possible for a general category
\mathcal{D}
. A possibly more feasible first step is to strictify (discrete)
\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k+1}
-spaces to
\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k+1}
-enriched categories in an efficient way that would then enable us to construct a direct Grothendieck construction.

One first advantage of a direct comparison is that we could better understand the relation between functors and universal
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibrations (
5.59
), rather than having to trace that relation through various equivalences (
5.52
).

(6)

(\infty,\infty)
-Cartesian Fibrations
We applied these results to simplicial presheaves on
\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k}
, which model
(\infty,n)
-categories. However, the results in
Section

4
are general enough to apply to simplicial presheaves on
\Theta_{\infty}
or other infinite versions of
\Theta_{k}\times{\mathbbe{\Delta}}^{n-k}
. The reason we stayed with finite diagrams is that there is no agreed upon notion of
(\infty,\infty)
-categories
using
\Theta_{\infty}
-spaces. Assuming that a theory of
(\infty,\infty)
-categories is developed using simplicial presheaves on some diagram, we expect the results here to immediately provide a theory of fibrations for
(\infty,\infty)
-categories.

(7)

Flagged
(\infty,n)
-Cartesian Fibrations:
In
[
AF18
]
Ayala and Francis define
flagged
(\infty,n)
-categories
and prove these are equivalent to Segal
\Theta_{n}
-spaces. The notion of a flagged
(\infty,n)
-category can be readily generalized to a notion of
flagged
(\infty,n)
-Cartesian fibration
. Given the equivalence due to Ayala and Francis, we should expect a generalization to an equivalence between Segal
(\infty,n)
-Cartesian fibrations (
5.16
) and flagged
(\infty,n)
-Cartesian fibrations.

(8)

Target Fibrations and Limits:
In
5.57
we prove that for every
(\infty,n)
-category
\mathscr{C}
, there is a
target
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration
with fiber over an object
c
in
\mathscr{C}
given by the over-category
\mathscr{C}_{/c}
. In the case of
(\infty,1)
-categories this coCartesian fibration is a Cartesian fibration if and only if
\mathscr{C}
has finite limits
[
Lur09a
, Lemma 6.1.1.1]
. On the other hand the theory of limits of
(\infty,n)
-categories is still in its early stages
[
GHL20a
]
and so this perspective suggests the following next step.

Assuming we have developed a theory of limits in a model of
(\infty,n)
-categories based on simplicial presheaves (such as
\Theta_{n}
-spaces), we want to prove that the target
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibration is a
(\infty,n)
-Cartesian fibration if and only if the
(\infty,n)
-category is finitely complete. Next, assuming we could prove a comparison theorem with fibrations in other models (as discussed above) we could then deduce the notion of being a finitely complete
(\infty,n)
-category is also model independent.

(9)

A model for the
(\infty,n)
-Category of
(\infty,n)
-Categories:

One common way for constructing quasi-categories is to apply the
simplicial nerve
to a Kan enriched category, which, in particular, can be obtained as the sub-category of fibrant-cofibrant objects of a simplicial model category
[
Lur09a
, Proposition 1.1.5.10]
. Using this approach we can in particular obtain the quasi-category of spaces from the Kan model structure or the quasi-category of
(\infty,1)
-categories from the complete Segal space model structure.

However, the simplicial nerve construction relies on the
strictification functor

\mathfrak{C}[-]
, which is computationally quite challenging (and a lot of effort has gone into a better understanding
[
Rie11
,
DS11
]
). So, for some important examples, such as spaces and
(\infty,1)
-categories, it is beneficial to have more direct constructions. For example a key aspect in the construction of the
simplicial model of univalent foundations
due to Kapulkin and Lumsdaine is an explicit construction of the
(\infty,0)
-category of
(\infty,0)
-categories, which is necessary to prove the existence of
univalent universes

[
KL12
]
.

One established method for the explicit construction of such
(\infty,1)
-categories is via
(\infty,1)
-coCartesian fibrations, examples of which can be found in
[
Cis19
]
for simplicial sets and
[
KV14
,
Ras18a
]
for simplicial spaces. Hence we expect that
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibrations can be used in a similar fashion to construct explicit models of
(\infty,n)
-categories by focusing on
(\infty,n)
-coCartesian fibrations over the appropriate diagram.

(10)

Internal
(\infty,n)
-Categories:
One of the benefits of
(\infty,1)
-Cartesian fibrations is that it enables us to effectively study
internal
\infty
-categories
via
complete Segal objects

[
Ras17a
]
. This in particular allows us to internalize an important result by Rezk
[
Rez01
, Proposition 7.6]
, that characterizes equivalences of complete Segal objects via Dwyer-Kan equivalences
[
Ras17a
, Theorem 3.13]
.

Having a theory of
(\infty,n)
-Cartesian fibrations we should be able to study
internal
(\infty,n)
-categories
in a similar manner using
\Theta_{n}
-objects
. This, in particular, could then play a crucial role in the study of univalence and
(\infty,n)
-topos theory (as already discussed in
Section

0.5
).
